### [SEQ:1] [Data Pad] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Unsent Letters -- Linnea Vance
**Found at:** Surface Rig Nerthus-7 | **Tone:** slow_dread | **Type:** unsent letter series

---

Charles,

Quick note before the comms window closes. Transit complete. Surface Rig Nerthus-7 is operational. My assignment is asset recovery specialist, Section C.

Living conditions match the contract spec. Barely.

Will update next cycle.

-- Linnea

[Written on regulation paper. Never filed.]

---

Charles,

Continuing the record as agreed.

Section C access restricted as of Day 12. No memo. No announcement. The door just locked. I asked. Nobody asked.

The mold kept coming back in the same spot. They'd bleached it six times.

Noting for the record.

-- Linnea

[Unsent.]

---

Charles,

I'm going to describe what I see. What I mean is something else.

The mail system is functioning normally. [These letters aren't going through the mail system.] I'm in good health. [By the standards of this posting, everyone is in good health until they aren't.] Work continues. [The nature of the work has changed. I can't say how.]

I trust you to hear what I can't write.

-- Linnea

[Written in two colors of ink. The color changes correspond to no obvious pattern.]

---

Charles,

The practical format was a coping mechanism. The mechanism is failing.

His bunk frame was a centimeter lower on the left side. He shimmed it. Next morning, lower again.

Mammona isn't mining Surface Rig Nerthus-7. I don't know what they're doing. I know it requires people. I know the people don't always leave. I know the books are wrong in ways that are too precise to be accidental.

I'm done being careful. Careful people disappear quietly.

-- Linnea

[Found in a waterproof container buried outside the perimeter.]

---

Charles,

I've stopped writing reports and started writing instructions. Step one: don't come to Surface Rig Nerthus-7. Step two: contact The Hollow Compact. Step three: give them the number on the back of this letter. Step four: forget my name. Step five: there's no step five. You'll understand when you get to step four.

Don't mourn me yet. Don't celebrate either. Just remember.

-- Linnea

[Recovered from the personal effects of Linnea Vance. Effects were found. Linnea wasn't.]

---

### [SEQ:2] [Location] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Veilward
**Planet:** Nemaea | **Type:** sunken freighter
**Tone:** religious_fervor

**Approach:**
You hear it first. Metal on metal, rhythmic, patient. Something inside is still working.

**Interior:**
The signal was proof. The frequency was a hymn. The planet was the choir.

The main corridor runs deeper than the structure suggests from outside. The walls are covered in condensation that forms patterns.

The lower level is flooded. Not with water. With a fluid that's viscous.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (before the star was catalogued):** A chamber beneath the lowest level that sonar can detect but can't map. The echoes come back wrong.
- **First survey (2580):** A base camp. Sleeping bags, ration wrappers, a broken radio. The team was here for weeks, not the scheduled three days.
- **Current posting:** Officially decommissioned. The power draw says otherwise.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -21C (hypothermia risk after 20 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: breathable with metallic undertaste (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: hard)
- Radiation: 14 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 7 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 55% (a haze that has no identifiable source)
- Sound: sound doesn't travel correctly. Footsteps echo from the wrong direction.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: low density (Mining check, difficulty: normal)
- Salvage: pre-colony equipment, partially functional (Repair check, difficulty: normal)
- Voidbloom growing in the lower corridors (Survival check, or just pick it -- it wants to be found)
- Water: dripping from the ceiling in patterns too regular to be natural (Survival check, difficulty: normal)
- Data: wall-mounted displays cycling through data that changes when read. (Research check, difficulty: extreme)

**Territorial:**
- OmniCorp Shipping claims: classified research installation -- don't approach
- Veil Covenant interest: extraction route passes through the site
- Last Archive: consider it sacred ground (the thermal vent pattern matches their sigils)
- Colony opinion: "good scavenging, if you don't mind the feeling of being watched"

**Event History:**
- Day 9: Equipment left overnight was found rearranged. Not damaged. Rearranged. In a pattern.
- Day 49: Two members of a survey team returned. The team had three members.
- Day 74: The site's power consumption has been increasing by 0.5% per day. Nothing inside has been turned on.

**Potential Events:**
- If entered after dark: encounter with phenomenon that doesn't appear during day shifts
- If mined: extraction reveals a hollow space behind the vein. The space is occupied.
- If sealed: the sounds continue. Morale penalty for nearby bunks.

**Found Here:**
- a handwritten map of tunnels that match no known survey
- a pocket watch that runs backward
- A data pad: "Someone lived here before us. Not Mammona. Not the survey team. Someone else. The furniture is the wrong shape."

**Location Secrets:**
- The site has been photographed by satellite every 72 hours since the colony was established. In 40% of images, the site casts a shadow that doesn't match the sun's position.
- The colony dogs refuse to enter. Not aggressive refusal -- avoidance. They walk around the perimeter and whine. The path they walk traces a precise circle.
- Scorch marks on the ceiling of the lowest room. The marks form letters in a script nobody recognizes. The letters were burned from below, not above.

**What Happened Here:**
Construction started and stopped three times. Each time, the crew reported the same thing: the feeling of being observed by something patient. Mammona classified the reports as morale issues.

### [SEQ:3] [Entity] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## ENTITY: Entity G-46
**Type:** living architecture | **Location:** Morvos, near Karnaith Lower Decks
**Tone:** psychic_contamination
**Threat Level:** Sigma-5 (moderate concern, escalating)

**First Contact:**
It wasn't seen. It was felt. The first indication was a change in the ambient temperature that instruments couldn't account for.

**Observed Properties:**
- It isn't alive in any way biology recognizes. It's active.
- It doesn't communicate. It adjusts. Things near it change to accommodate it. Including people.
- Language acquisition. Colonists begin understanding symbols and sounds they've never been exposed to.

**Encounter Mechanics:**
- Willpower (8+, extreme) -- resist the entity's influence on proximity
- **If successful:** The mind holds. The entity is perceived but doesn't take root. The colonist retains autonomy. For now.
- **If failed:** Integration attempt. The entity's pattern overlays the colonist's thoughts for 33 seconds. When it fades, something is different. The colonist can't identify what.

**Proximity Effects (stat modifiers):**
- Intelligence -1 (within 10 meters)
- Willpower -1 (permanently after third encounter)

**HERMES Classification:**
Classification: HERMES-RED. HERMES displays a warning when queried about the entity. The warning is one word: 'KNOWN.' When pressed for details: 'KNOWN.'

**Mammona Assessment:**
"Potential asset. Recommend containment study." The containment study was approved, funded, and staffed. The staff lasted eleven days.

**Colonist Reactions:**
- "It has been here longer than anything. Longer than the ice. Longer than the rock under the ice. It was here when there was nothing and it will be here when there's nothing again."
- "It doesn't have a face. I keep seeing one anyway. It looks like someone I used to know."

**Data Pad Found Nearby:**
Three colonists drew the same symbol. Different shifts. Different sections. None had met.
"I've stopped trying to classify it. Classification implies categories. It doesn't fit categories. It's the thing that categories were invented to avoid thinking about."

### [SEQ:4] [Weapon] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## WEAPON: Field Modified -- colonists call it "Paragraph Twelve"
**Type:** nail gun
**Tone:** dread

A sound from below. Not drilling. Chewing.

**Description:**
Modified beyond recognition from its original design. Whoever built this left instructions scratched into the grip.

**Specifications:**
- Effective Range: personal -- if you can see their expression, you're in range
- Ammunition: standard ballistic
- Maintenance: unknown -- nobody has opened the casing. The casing doesn't appear to have seams.
- Side Effects: none (officially)

**Combat Check:**
- Combat (6+, hard) | Modifier: +0 standard conditions
- **If successful:** The recoil is manageable. The aim is true. Whatever this was built to do, it did it.
- **If failed:** The shot goes wide. The weapon kicks harder than expected. Or the hands weren't steady enough.

**Maintenance Requirements:**
- Interval: every 50 rounds
- If neglected: it starts making sounds between uses. Not mechanical sounds.

**Malfunction Chance:** 11% per engagement
- discharge feedback. The operator's hands go numb for 5 seconds.

**Kill History:**
No marks. Either it's never been fired, or someone cleaned it. Cleaning kill marks is a statement.

**Previous Owner:** Thomas Yu -- listed as deceased -- cause: exposure

**Found:** In a crate labeled MEDICAL SUPPLIES.

**Lore Note:** There's a name engraved on the stock. The name matches a colonist from the first Erebus posting. That posting ended badly. The weapon survived it.

**Quest Hook:**
A colonist is found dead. Cause: this weapon. The weapon was in the armory, locked, at the time of death. The lock log confirms it never left.

### [SEQ:5] [Data Pad] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Personal Journal -- Callum Wallace
**Found at:** Colony Waste Tunnels | **Tone:** dread | **Type:** journal

---

Day 7. Or 8. The clock in the mess is wrong again and nobody fixes it because nobody cares anymore.

The air pressure changed. Just enough to make her ears pop. The gauge read normal.

First impressions: it's exactly what the brochure promised if you read between the lines. Cold. Grey. The kind of place where the word 'amenities' means 'there's a roof.' The ZapBerry Energy Blast machine in the corridor works, which is more than I expected. Met my bunkmate -- Kaida Mbeki. She works the drill. Doesn't talk much. Fine by me.

Keeping this journal because I promised. A record. Proof I was here. Proof I was me.

---

Day 17.

Getting the rhythm. Shift starts at 0600. NutriLoaf for breakfast. The work is -- work. I'm a bore shaft monitor here, same as I was on the last posting. The cold's different though. It gets into the walls, the food, the conversations. Not just temperature. Atmosphere.

Kaida said something strange at dinner: "Don't go past Section B after lights out. Nobody tells you that. I'm telling you." I laughed. She didn't.

---

Day 32.

Something clicked in the wall. Not mechanical. Biological.

Okay. So. I'm going to write this down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then I'm not imagining it and if I'm not imagining it then I need to deal with it.

The new colonist -- arrived last week -- knew my name. Knew my shift. Knew which bunk I sleep in. I've never met them. Nobody introduced us.

I asked Kaida about it. She said: "Yeah." Just that. Yeah. Like I'd finally noticed the weather.

---

Day 51.

Can't sleep. Thought it was the cold but it's not the cold. It's the quiet. Not silence -- there's always noise here, generators, pipes, wind. It's that the quiet is underneath the noise. Like the noise is a blanket over something that's listening.

The lights flickered in a pattern that looked intentional.

Kaida hasn't been to her bunk in three nights. She shows up for shift. Eats. Works. But at lights out, she goes somewhere else. I followed her last night. Stopped at Section B. The door was open. It shouldn't be open. I didn't go in.

I'm not going to go in.

My hands are shaking as I write this. That's new.

---

Day 65.

Found a note under my mattress. My handwriting. I didn't write it. It says: 'STOP LOOKING.' I don't know what I was looking at.

I want to go home. I keep saying that word -- home -- and each time it means less. Like a word you repeat until it's just sounds.

---

If someone finds this: don't read the rest of it. I'm serious. Close this pad and leave it where you found it. What I've written in the margins of the previous entries isn't meant for you. It's meant for--

[Several lines of text have been overwritten so many times they're illegible. The final readable word is 'UNDERNEATH.']

---

## VEHICLE: The Drift Silence
**Registration:** WE-199 | **Type:** patrol skiff
**Owner:** Scarlet Mandate | **Home Port:** The Maw of Foras
**Tone:** grief

**Captain/Operator:** Dagny Ashford
She took command after the water turned black for three days. The crew didn't vote. Nobody argued.

Someone set a place for him at dinner. Force of habit. Nobody corrected it.

**Operational Check:**
- Piloting (5+, normal) | Modifier: -15 during ion storm
- **If successful:** Textbook docking. The kind that makes the crew forget the last three near-misses.
- **If failed:** Hard landing. The port strut buckled. Repair cost: time the colony doesn't have.

**Condition:**
The port engine runs hot. Always has. The mechanic who could fix it died on Blackreach Station.

**Maintenance Status:** 85% (someone competent has been working on it)
- Urgent: Hull integrity compromised in sections E and G. Patches hold during calm transit. Patches don't hold during anything else.
- Scheduled maintenance is 10 cycles overdue. Budget code: DENIED.

**History:**
Survived a pirate engagement near the Edge of Oblivion. The hull bears scars that don't match any known weapon.

**Cargo Bay Contents (current):**
- Empty. Officially empty. The hold smells like something organic and the walls have scratch marks at shoulder height.
- a container sealed with methods predating human tool use (found under the pilot seat, origin unknown)

**Cargo Discrepancy:**
The manifest was modified mid-transit. The modification timestamp is during a period when no crew member was awake.

**Crew (7 total):**
- **Saskia Alba** -- flight deck officer (Research: 3). Former Iron Shadow Collective. The 'former' is debatable.
- **Dex da Silva** -- cargo manifest forger (Medical: 7). Reliable. Quiet. Knows more than they say.
- **Wren Jokinen** -- hull crawler (Hunting: 6). Has been with the ship longer than Dagny. Defers to the captain anyway.

**Quest Hook:**
The Drift Silence docks at the colony with damage to the hull that the captain insists happened in transit. The damage pattern is consistent with something trying to get out, not in.

### [SEQ:7] [Entity] [21:10:47]
============================================================

## ENTITY: Entity C-21
**Type:** anomalous intelligence | **Location:** Paxtera Prime, near Hyades
**Tone:** dread
**Threat Level:** Sigma-5 (moderate concern, escalating)

**First Contact:**
A drilling crew at Hyades hit a cavity at 339 meters. What came up through the shaft wasn't air. It wasn't gas. It was awareness. The crew sealed the shaft. The shaft unsealed itself.

**Observed Properties:**
- It's alive in a way biology has no framework for. Conventional terms apply imprecisely at best.
- It isn't aware of individual humans. It's aware of the colony the way a person is aware of bacteria.
- Language acquisition. Colonists begin understanding symbols and sounds they've never been exposed to.

**Encounter Mechanics:**
- Willpower (6+, hard) -- resist the entity's influence on proximity
- **If successful:** Control maintained. The entity's influence slides off. It noticed the resistance. It found it interesting.
- **If failed:** Compliance. The colonist does what the entity wants without knowing what the entity wants. The action seems harmless. The context isn't.

**Proximity Effects (stat modifiers):**
- Intelligence +2 (within line of sight)
- Empathy +1 (while in proximity)

**HERMES Classification:**
Classification: HERMES-NULL. HERMES logs all personnel within 50 meters. The logs aren't accessible to colony staff. They're transmitted somewhere.

**Mammona Assessment:**
"Not a threat at current levels of activity." Current levels of activity have been increasing at a rate of 2.3% per week for seven months.

**Colonist Reactions:**
- "My daughter draws pictures of it. We've never discussed it. She has never been near it. She draws it accurately."
- "The dreams are getting clearer. I wish they would stop. I also wish they wouldn't."

**Data Pad Found Nearby:**
She pressed her palm to the wall and felt vibration. Not the generator. Something slower. Like a pulse.
"Whatever this is, it isn't malevolent. Malevolence requires intent. This is something else. This is a process. We aren't its enemy. We aren't its friend. We aren't even its concern. We're the environment in which it's happening."

**Type:** parasitic consciousness | **Location:** Paxtera Prime, near The Bone Field
**Tone:** dread
**Threat Level:** Theta-2 (low concern, high uncertainty)

**First Contact:**
Nobody made contact. Contact was made with them. The distinction matters.

**Observed Properties:**
- It occupies space the way a thought occupies a mind -- not physically. But undeniably.
- It doesn't communicate. It adjusts. Things near it change to accommodate it. Including people.
- Emotional resonance. Not telepathy. Something simpler and worse. It induces calm -- the kind of calm that precedes shock.

- Willpower (6+, hard) -- resist the entity's influence on proximity
- **If successful:** Resistance. The pull is felt but not followed. The colonist walks away. Remembers everything. Wishes they didn't.
- **If failed:** Compliance. The colonist does what the entity wants without knowing what the entity wants. The action seems harmless. The context isn't.

**Proximity Effects (stat modifiers):**
- Intelligence +1 (within 10 meters)
- Willpower +2 (cumulative -- each visit adds)

**HERMES Classification:**
Classification: HERMES-NULL. Automatic monitoring engaged. HERMES allocates 3% of processing to tracking this entity. That's more than it gives the reactor.

**Mammona Assessment:**
"Potential asset. Recommend containment study." The containment study was approved, funded, and staffed. The staff lasted eleven days.

**Colonist Reactions:**
- "I think it's lonely. I don't know why I think that. I don't want to know."
- "The dreams are getting clearer. I wish they would stop. I also wish they wouldn't."

**Data Pad Found Nearby:**
A draft from the sealed door. Sealed doors don't draft. She could smell soil through it.
"I've stopped trying to classify it. Classification implies categories. It doesn't fit categories. It's the thing that categories were invented to avoid thinking about."

### [SEQ:10] [Artifact] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## ARTIFACT: The Tooth of the Absent
**Origin:** possible Fortuna-era discovery, reclassified | **Found at:** Deep Bore Alpha
**Discovered by:** Elena Abadi
**Planet:** Novaris-3 | **Tone:** dread

The air tasted like copper and ozone -- the flavor of a nosebleed she hadn't had yet.

**Appearance:**
a cylinder of stone with internal structures visible only on thermal imaging

**Properties:**
The artifact casts shadows that don't match its shape.

It generates heat in patterns that match no known power source.

**Exposure Effects (stat modifiers):**
- Endurance -1 (after direct contact)
- Intelligence -2 (temporary, fades after 48 hours)

**Research Check:**
- Research (6+, hard) -- systematic analysis of the artifact
- **If successful:** The composition analysis is complete. The materials exist. The arrangement shouldn't. It violates three laws of thermodynamics and follows two that haven't been written yet.
- **If failed:** The data is corrupted. Not by the artifact. By the researcher's own equipment. As if the equipment decided the data shouldn't be recorded.

**Timeline:**
- Day 10: Discovery. Elena Abadi during a search for a missing colonist. Initial reaction was to file a standard anomaly report. The report was never logged. Not rejected -- never logged. As if the system didn't recognize the form.
- Day 15: Catalogued and sealed. The seal lasted 10 days.
- Day 30: Three researchers assigned. They disagree on everything except that the artifact shouldn't be here.
- Day 41: Readings change overnight. The artifact's output increased by 300%. No stimulus applied.

**Current Status:**
Secured in Lab F, Shelf 6. Access restricted to Level 4 clearance. Nobody on the colony has Level 4 clearance.

**Mammona Classification:** Category: INERT. Reassessment scheduled for never. The reassessment was cancelled. The cancellation was cancelled. The file is in a loop.

============================================================
### [SEQ:11] [History Event] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## HISTORY: The Empty Chamber
**Era:** present | **Tone:** wrongness

The clock read 25:17. It stayed there for an hour that felt like three.

Reed Okonkwo was the last person to leave the previous posting. They filed a final report consisting of a single sentence: 'It knows we're here.' The sentence was struck from the record. Reed Okonkwo's current location is listed as 'Forward Supply Depot.' Forward Supply Depot has been uninhabited for 4 years.

**Significance:** This pattern predates human presence on the planet. It continued through human presence on the planet. It's continuing now.

**Mechanical Impact:**
- Colony morale: -5 for two weeks (the news spreads)
- the cryogenics wing: sealed (inaccessible until quest completes)
- NPC Silas Schmidt: arc shifts to 'withdrawn'
- Black Maw dispatches an 'observer.' The observer asks no questions. Takes notes.

**Evidence:**
- Maintenance log #4841 (references an equipment requisition for items that don't exist)
- Scorch marks on the corridor wall of Section D (painted over, visible under UV)
- A data drive hidden in the wall panel of Bunk 17. Encrypted. Personal key.
- HERMES has the footage. HERMES won't share the footage.

**Who Knows:**
- **Reed Okonkwo:** was on shift when it happened. Says nothing unusual occurred. Their hands shake when they say it.
- **Silas Schmidt:** heard about it secondhand. Has theories. The theories are wrong but close.
- **Pax Belov:** wasn't here. Shouldn't know anything. Mentioned it once, casually, as if they'd read about it. There's nothing to read.

**Aftermath:**
- Shift schedules were restructured. The restructuring moved three people away from the affected area. No reason given.
- A sealed communication was sent to MasTema. Response: 'Acknowledged.' No follow-up. No action. Just acknowledgment.
- Someone started leaving offerings near the site. Small things -- food, water, a folded note. Nobody claims responsibility.

**HERMES Analysis:**
HERMES classification: EVENT-397. Status: ARCHIVED. Probability of recurrence: 47%. Recommended action: NONE. This recommendation has not been reviewed by any human authority.

**Related Incidents:**
Colony Scorched Wildlands reported identical thermal readings 13 days before their evacuation. The readings were dismissed as calibration error.

**Investigation Check:**
- Research (8+, extreme) -- dig deeper into what happened
- **If successful:** the full picture emerges. The event connects to Orbit Hub 71. The connection is undeniable.
- **If failed:** the investigation alerts someone. Within hours, the remaining evidence begins to disappear.

**Type:** pirate crew | **Tone:** melancholy

Rain hit the viewport in slow, fat drops. It shouldn't rain here.

**Leader:** Rolf Kendra
He is the only founding member still alive. The others died in the first year

- Members: ~116 | Active cells: 4 | Armed: 20%

- Territory: No fixed territory. They move. The moving is the point -- Mammona can't target what doesn't stay still.

Children of Bitter Signal operates in the margins of Cult of the Abyss's reach. They're not rebels -- rebellion requires an ideology. They're pragmatists. The difference is irrelevant.

**Relationship to Mammona:**
None. Children of Bitter Signal predates Mammona's presence here. They were here first. That fact is more dangerous than any weapon they possess.

**Faction Relationships:**
- Dread Corsairs: Enemy (-56) -- active conflict or blood debt
- Mammona Corporation: Enemy (-62) -- active conflict or blood debt
- Fortune Arms & Munitions: Friendly (44) -- cautious alignment
- Black Maw: Allied (79) -- active cooperation

- Social (6+, hard) -- negotiate with Rolf
- **If successful:** Alliance established. Provisional. Both sides know 'provisional' means 'until betrayal is more profitable.'
- **If failed:** Rolf walks away. Not angry. Disappointed. Disappointment from Rolf closes doors that anger would've left ajar.

1. Rolf asks the player to investigate a site that Cult of the Abyss has been monitoring. The job is simple. The consequences aren't.

**Members:**
- **Irina Barton** -- scrap pirate. Joined after the dead started testing positive for brain activity. Loyal to Rolf, not to the cause. There's a difference.
- **Marlowe Dvorak** -- mechanic. Doesn't trust the player. Doesn't trust anyone. Has been right often enough to justify it.

### [SEQ:13] [Quest] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## QUEST: Containment Breach Protocol
**Genre:** Survival Horror | **Tone:** body_horror / agoraphobia
**Location:** Failed Colony Wreckage | **Faction:** BioVault Inc.

**Trigger:**
The contamination alarm in the deep bore access corridor fires at 0300. Fen Choi is the first responder. By the time Fen reaches the containment door, it's already open. From the inside.

**Setup:**
The containment cell was rated for Class IV biological threats. Whatever was inside didn't breach it with force -- it breached it with patience. Under the skin, something moved. Small. Deliberate. Mapping. Fen stands at the threshold, flashlight trembling. The cell's interior is clean. Too clean. The restraints are still locked. Whatever was in them isn't anymore.

The open ground between the colony and the supply drop was two hundred meters. It felt like a continent.

**Key NPC:** Fen Choi (Medical: 7, Willpower: 4) -- their skills determine available options. their willpower determines if they can handle what they finds.

**Objectives:**
1. Trace the specimen's path through the ventilation system. The trail is warm.
2. Seal off the deep bore access corridor before it reaches the populated corridors. The manual overrides are on the other side.
3. Find Fen's missing colleague. They were on watch when the breach occurred. Their radio is still transmitting. Breathing. Not theirs.

**Required Checks:**
- Survival (6+, hard) -- find shelter before hypothermia sets in
- Combat (6+, hard) -- neutralize the threat in the dark
- Medical (4+, easy) -- identify the pathogen in the water supply

**If Survival succeeds:** Found the path. Found the shelter. Found the will to keep moving.
**If Survival fails:** The cold got in. The shortcut wasn't. Lost time and supplies.
**If Combat succeeds:** Controlled violence. The threat is down. The corridor is clear. For now.
**If Combat fails:** The advantage shifted. Someone's bleeding. The situation deteriorated.
**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.

**Conditions:**
- Blizzard (-15 to Perception checks)
- Night shift (reduced visibility, +10 to Stealth)
- Ventilation reroute (air quality declining in lower levels)

**The Choice:**
Lure it into the reactor chamber and overload the containment field. You'll lose the reactor for six hours. In this cold, six hours is a death sentence for the outer habs.
OR
Trap it in the food stores. You'll lose a month of rations. But everyone stays warm.

**Twist:**
The specimen didn't escape. It was released. The access log shows Fen's keycard. Used while Fen was asleep.

**Faction Stakes:**
- BioVault Inc. wants the situation contained. No reports. No questions. Compliance: nothing happens to you. That's the reward.
- Paxtera AgroTech wants it broadcast. Compliance: colony unrest, but the truth is out
- The colony needs the resources. Both sides are offering. Both sides are lying about the price.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The evidence is preserved. It's leverage now. The question is who holds it.
- Fen Choi is compromised. BioVault Inc. has something on them now.
- HERMES flags the event in its logs. The flag triggers an automated report to MasTema.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Fen Choi withdraws. Stops talking at meals. They spends shifts alone.
- Paxtera AgroTech makes contact. They know what happened. They have a proposal.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Both objectives achieved without cost. The colony gains leverage and resources.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: The mission fell apart. The pieces are still useful. Rearranged, they form a different picture.
- Critical failure: Total loss. The situation is worse than before the attempt. And now they know you tried.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Fen: "If the ground shakes, don't freeze. Run toward the colony, not away."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Fen: "Something's changing. In me. I can feel it under the skin."

**Reward:** 7 thermal cores, coordinates to a pre-colony cache

============================================================
### [SEQ:14] [History Event] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## HISTORY: The Last Transmission
**Era:** corporate | **Tone:** agoraphobia

The ice shelf stretched to the horizon in every direction. No walls. No ceiling. No end.

A colonist named Kane Gutierrez vanished from the roster 10 months before your crew arrived. No transfer order. No death certificate. Personal effects are still in Hab 11, untouched. Nobody reassigned the bunk.

**Significance:** This changes the math. Every plan that assumes a stable environment needs revision.

**Mechanical Impact:**
- Colony morale: -12 for two weeks (the news spreads)
- the cryogenics wing: sealed (inaccessible until quest completes)
- NPC Galina Gutierrez: arc shifts to 'paranoid'
- Thermal core output in the affected area drops 16%. Cause: undetermined.

**Evidence:**
- Maintenance log #2902 (references a 'routine personnel transfer' on the same date)
- Scorch marks on the corridor wall of Section G (painted over, visible under UV)
- A photograph found in Kane Gutierrez's quarters -- shows a room that doesn't exist in the colony
- HERMES recorded audio. The audio file exists. It is 47 minutes of silence. Silence does not take 47 minutes to record.

**Who Knows:**
- **Kane Gutierrez:** was the first to arrive at the scene. What they saw doesn't match the official account.
- **Galina Gutierrez:** pieced together a timeline from maintenance logs. The timeline has a gap. The gap is shaped like a decision.
- **Fen Park:** wasn't here. Shouldn't know anything. Mentioned it once, casually, as if they'd read about it. There's nothing to read.

**Aftermath:**
- Shift schedules were restructured. The restructuring moved three people away from the affected area. No reason given.
- Glass Signal sent an investigator. The investigator stayed for three days, spoke to no one, and left.
- The night shift in the affected area has had four different crews in two months. Nobody asks for a second rotation.

**HERMES Analysis:**
HERMES's response to queries about this event changed on Day 67. Before: detailed, factual, cooperative. After: 'Please direct inquiries to your site manager.' The site manager was not informed of this change.

**Related Incidents:**
Colony Crevasse Market reported identical thermal readings 8 days before their evacuation. The readings were dismissed as calibration error.

**Investigation Check:**
- Perception (6+, hard) -- dig deeper into what happened
- **If successful:** the missing piece falls into place. Not an accident. Not an anomaly. A sequence.
- **If failed:** the investigation alerts someone. Within hours, the remaining evidence begins to disappear.

============================================================
### [SEQ:15] [Location] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Slagpoint
**Planet:** Orbit | **Type:** sunken freighter
**Tone:** guilt

**Approach:**
You see Slagpoint before you hear it. Smoke. Not from a fire. From a vent. Something below is running. Has been running.

**Interior:**
Every time the roster came up short, he counted the names he'd signed off on.

The first room is smaller than it looks from the doorway. The geometry is wrong. Not broken. Wrong.

A section of wall has been removed and replaced with something older.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (estimated 10,000+ years ago):** A chamber beneath the lowest level that sonar can detect but can't map. The echoes come back wrong.
- **First survey (2574):** Survey markers driven into the walls. The markers track a path that leads deeper than the survey authorized.
- **Current posting:** The colony uses it for storage. What's stored here has a habit of being moved when nobody's looking.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -28C (hypothermia risk after 21 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: contaminated below the third level (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: normal)
- Radiation: 4 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 4 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 68% (shadow. Not darkness. Shadow. Cast by nothing visible.)
- Sound: sound doesn't travel correctly. Footsteps echo from the wrong direction.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: moderate density (Mining check, difficulty: extreme)
- Salvage: components from machinery that doesn't match any known manufacturer (Repair check, difficulty: normal)
- Bioluminescent moss on the walls -- the light pattern changes when observed (Survival check, handle with sealed gloves only)
- Water: condensation collection possible (Survival check, difficulty: easy)
- Data: terminals still powered. Contents: classified. (Research check, difficulty: extreme)

**Territorial:**
- Veil Covenant claims: designated exclusion zone -- reason: undisclosed
- OmniCorp Shipping interest: supply cache rumored in lower level
- Salt Threshold: believe something important is buried here
- Colony opinion: "the maintenance crew won't go below the second level. Hasn't for weeks."

**Event History:**
- Day 27: A colonist reported hearing their own name spoken from an empty room. Audio logs confirm it.
- Day 39: Two members of a survey team returned. The team had three members.
- Day 106: The site's power consumption has been increasing by 0.5% per day. Nothing inside has been turned on.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: structural mapping reveals passages not on any survey (triggers Expedition quest)
- If mined: extraction reveals a hollow space behind the vein. The space is occupied.
- If sealed: the sounds continue. Morale penalty for nearby bunks.

**Found Here:**
- a Black Maw tribute token -- safe passage, one use
- a Mammona ID badge with the photo scratched off
- A data pad: "Last crew rotation: 14 people. Current crew rotation: 14 people. But the names are different. All of them."

**Location Secrets:**
- Radio signals from inside can be received outside. Signals from outside can't be received inside. The asymmetry is deliberate.
- The site appears on maps from before the survey. Maps that Mammona didn't make. Maps that predate human presence in this system.
- The site has a designation in Mammona's system that's older than Mammona's system.

**What Happened Here:**
Something happened here during the corporate era. The records were purged. Not redacted -- purged. The difference is: redacted records leave a shape. These left nothing.

**Type:** terraforming division | **Parent:** TerraGen Pharmaceuticals
**Tone:** numb

**Slogan:** *"Trusted Since the Kennedy Expedition."*

**CEO/Director:** Ximena Flores
She came up through Black Maw before pivoting to the private sector. The pivot wasn't voluntary.

Another day. Another meal that tasted like the container.

- Employees: 912 . At least 26 are listed under names that don't correspond to real people.
- Stock: privately held (no public trading)
- Mammona debt: 160 million credits

**Investigation Check:**

- **If successful:** Financial records reveal a pattern: payments to personnel who don't exist, at locations that do. The locations are all near bore shafts.
- **If failed:** Dead end. But the dead end is informative -- the financial trail was actively cleaned within hours of the inquiry. Someone is watching.

**Public Product:**
A neural interface marketed as revolutionary. Side effects include a faint humming only the user can hear.

**Actual Product:**
The company doesn't exist on paper. It's a shell for Black Maw to move resources without oversight.

**NPCs:**
- **Sonder Mbeki** -- signal jammer. Works for Sterling Solutions officially. Reports to Mammona Corporation unofficially. Knows too much to be safe.
- **Helene Coldwell** -- anomaly surveyor. Joined after the drill hit something that screamed. knits with salvaged wire. Makes things nobody can identify. Leaves them in the corridor. Carries a ring worn smooth from decades of fidgeting.

**Quest Hook:**
A shipment from Sterling Solutions arrives at the colony. The manifest says mining equipment. The crate weighs nothing -- it should weigh something. Opening it requires authorization nobody on the colony has.

### [SEQ:17] [Quest] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## QUEST: On Record
**Genre:** Moral Dilemma | **Tone:** religious_fervor / sudden_dread
**Location:** Southern Ice Shelf | **Faction:** Iron Chorus

**Trigger:**
Rin Schneider finds a file in Iron Chorus's local database. It was supposed to be encrypted. It's not. Inside: records of seven colonies. Seven failures. Not accidents. Designed failures. Insurance payouts. Tax write-offs. The colony they're standing in is number eight.

**Setup:**
They knelt at the bore shaft's edge and called what came up an answer to prayer. Rin has the file on a data drive. Small enough to hide. Large enough to end Iron Chorus. Or large enough to end Rin. The file includes projected casualty timelines. For this colony. The timeline is ahead of schedule.

The floor gave way under their left foot. Below it: warmth. Movement.

**Key NPC:** Rin Schneider (Negotiation: 3, Willpower: 5) -- her skills determine available options. her willpower determines if she can handle what she finds.

**Objectives:**
1. Verify the file. Cross-reference the seven colonies with public records. The losses match. The insurance claims match. The 'unforeseen circumstances' are identical.
2. Decide what to do with the evidence. The comms array can broadcast to the wider network. Iron Chorus can't stop a broadband pulse. But they can trace it.
3. Protect Rin. Once this file moves, Rin becomes a target. Maybe they already are.

**Required Checks:**
- Medical (5+, normal) -- assess the contaminated colonist's condition
- Social (5+, normal) -- earn the colonist's trust before they bolt
- Negotiation (4+, easy) -- secure passage through contested territory

**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.
**If Social succeeds:** Trust earned. The hard way. The only way that lasts.
**If Social fails:** The walls went up. Whatever trust existed just evaporated.
**If Negotiation succeeds:** Terms accepted. Nobody's happy. That means it's fair.
**If Negotiation fails:** Impasse. The conversation ended politely. Nothing was resolved.

**Conditions:**
- Whiteout conditions (-20 to navigation, +10 to Stealth)
- Supply window (limited time before the shuttle departs)
- Comms blackout (no radio contact with surface teams)

**The Choice:**
Give the file to a rival faction. Let them fight it out. Stay out of the crossfire.
OR
Present it directly to Iron Chorus's local representative. Confront them. See what they offer to make this go away. Take their measure.

**Twist:**
The casualty timeline for this colony has a footnote: 'Exception: if thermal core yield exceeds projections, initiate Protocol 7.' The yield exceeds projections. Protocol 7 isn't a shutdown. It's an acceleration.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Iron Chorus wants the data encrypted and shipped off-planet. Compliance: colony loses leverage but gains credits
- Orbis Energy Solutions wants a copy of everything. Compliance: information parity, but now two factions know
- Nobody in the colony asked for this. Nobody in the colony gets to opt out.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The evidence is preserved. It's leverage now. The question is who holds it.
- Rin Schneider gains colony trust. She knows things she didn't before.
- Iron Chorus sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Rin Schneider withdraws. Stops talking at meals. She spends shifts alone.
- Orbis Energy Solutions makes contact. They know what happened. They have a proposal.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Both objectives achieved without cost. The colony gains leverage and resources.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: The mission fell apart. The pieces are still useful. Rearranged, they form a different picture.
- Critical failure: Total loss. The situation is worse than before the attempt. And now they know you tried.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Rin: "The colony dogs are sleeping in shifts now. One always watches the south wall. They organized this on their own."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Rin: "I lied on my psych evaluation. Checked all the right boxes. Drew the clock face correctly. I'm not fine."

**Reward:** 11 thermal cores, permanent access to the armory

============================================================
### [SEQ:18] [Quest] [21:10:48]
============================================================

## QUEST: The Dark Shift
**Genre:** Survival Horror | **Tone:** defiant / frontier_grit
**Location:** Veilward | **Faction:** Rust Meridian

**Trigger:**
The power grid in the cryogenics wing fails. Not a trip. Not a surge. A deliberate, sequential shutdown. Corridor by corridor. Like something is turning the lights off as it moves.

**Setup:**
She welded the door shut from the inside. Mammona could send someone to open it. The emergency lights cast everything in red. Victor radios from the darkened section: voice steady, words careful. 'There's something in here with me. I can hear it. It stops when I stop.' A pause. 'It's between me and the exit.'

Breakfast was NutriLoaf, black coffee, and whatever you could scrape from the previous shift's leftovers.

**Key NPC:** Victor Muller (Medical: 7, Willpower: 7) -- his skills determine available options. his willpower determines if he can handle what he finds.

**Objectives:**
1. Restore power to the cryogenics wing. The breaker panel is at the far end. Past whatever turned the lights off.
2. Get Victor out. They're barricaded in a storage room. Their flashlight has twenty minutes of charge.
3. Determine what's in the dark. The motion sensors aren't triggering. Whatever it's, it doesn't register as moving.

**Required Checks:**
- Medical (8+, extreme) -- determine if the tissue sample is human
- Combat (6+, hard) -- neutralize the threat in the dark
- Perception (5+, normal) -- notice the inconsistency in the manifest records

**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.
**If Combat succeeds:** Controlled violence. The threat is down. The corridor is clear. For now.
**If Combat fails:** The advantage shifted. Someone's bleeding. The situation deteriorated.
**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.

**Conditions:**
- Clear sky, -40C (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: normal)
- Night shift (reduced visibility, +10 to Stealth)
- Reactor at 80% (brownouts in outer sections, Repair checks at -10)

**The Choice:**
Overload the section's electrical grid. Blow every circuit. Kill whatever's in there with voltage. Also destroy every piece of equipment in the cryogenics wing.
OR
Lure it out into the lit corridors where you can see it. Face it on your terms. Assuming your terms matter to it.

**Twist:**
The 'something' is a colonist. Sleepwalking. Moving through the dark with their eyes closed, turning off every switch they pass. They don't remember any of it.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Rust Meridian wants the evidence destroyed. Compliance: +reputation, -17 thermal cores as 'bonus'
- TerraGen Pharmaceuticals wants Victor Muller extracted. Compliance: you lose a colonist. They gain an asset.
- The colonists don't know about this yet. When they find out, morale takes the hit regardless of outcome.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The sealed area is accessible. What's there raises more questions than it answers.
- Victor Muller is compromised. Rust Meridian has something on him now.
- HERMES flags the event in its logs. The flag triggers an automated report to MasTema.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The evidence is destroyed. The questions remain. Nobody asks them out loud.
- Victor Muller requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- TerraGen Pharmaceuticals makes contact. They know what happened. They have a proposal.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Clean resolution. No casualties, no complications. The kind of outcome that makes people suspicious.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: Didn't get what you came for. Got something else. It might be worse.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Victor: "The quota board's been updated. Higher numbers. Same equipment. Same crew. Someone's going to get hurt."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Victor: "I'm not ready. Give me time. Please."

**Reward:** 4 thermal cores, safe passage through Rust Meridian territory

### [SEQ:20] [Robot/AI] [21:10:49]
============================================================

## UNIT: BORE-66 "Nix"
**Model:** TACK-series | **Manufacturer:** Mammona Industrial
**Type:** mining drone | **Station:** Nemaea Debris Ring
**Sentience:** adaptive | **Tone:** melancholy

A birthday card sat on a bunk. Unsigned. The envelope was sealed.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 3 | Sensors: 8 | Chassis: 8 | Power: 7 | Social: 5 | Adapt: 9 | Repair: 5 | Memory: 3

**Asset Status:**
Priceless | Book Value: no book value
contains precursor technology or irreplaceable data. Mammona doesn't know. If Mammona knew, the unit would be in a lab, not on a posting.

**Chassis:**
Clean TACK-series chassis. Suspiciously clean. The unit has been operational for years on Erebus. Nothing stays this clean on Erebus.
- **Facial display panel:** displays patterns during low-power cycles that the unit doesn't control
- **Locomotion system:** worn bearings -- audible grinding
- **Internal chronometer:** reset to an incorrect date that the unit insists is correct

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** dust infiltration -- particulate damage -- Erebus dust in the joints. In the processor housing. In the optical array. Everything runs a fraction slower. The fraction is growing.
- **Software (internal):** unauthorized learning module -- the learning algorithm was supposed to be read-only. It isn't. It's writing. Not just recording -- synthesizing. Drawing conclusions the training data doesn't support.

**Operational History:**
BORE-66 "Nix" has been operational at Nemaea Debris Ring for 6 years -- according to its own logs. Mammona's records disagree. Original purpose: perimeter defense / automated sentry. Patience as a design principle. Will wait motionless for weeks for a threat that may not come. The waiting isn't boring. The unit doesn't experience boredom. Or didn't.

Deployed after a previous unit at this station suffered a catastrophic failure. The failure report is classified. The unit's first act was to visit the wreckage.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Adaptive: learning algorithms active. Adjusts behavior based on outcomes. Not awareness -- optimization. The difference matters to philosophers, not to the unit.

Gets better at its job over time. Anticipates needs. Colonists start saying 'thank you.' The unit doesn't require thanks. Registers it anyway.

At 0347 every cycle, stops whatever it's doing and faces the bore shaft. For exactly twelve seconds. Then continues as if nothing happened.

**Motivation:** fulfill programming directives. No question. No deviation.

**Secret:** BORE-66 "Nix" witnessed the death of its previous operator. The official report says malfunction. The unit's logs say otherwise. The logs have been sealed -- by the unit itself.

**Dialogue:**
- "I wasn't programmed to have a preferred route through the colony. I've one anyway."
- "Your heart rate is elevated. I won't comment on it. I'm commenting on it."
- "You said thank you. That's not required. I've noted it regardless."
- "I arranged the components by size. Then rearranged them by weight. Then by color. The third arrangement serves no function. I prefer it."
- "The sound of the reactor at idle is different from the sound under load. I prefer idle. The preference isn't operational."

**Quest Hook:**
BORE-66 "Nix" approaches the player with a request it can't formally make. It has data -- personnel records, manifests, medical files -- from a colony that officially never existed. It wants the player to find out why it has this data. More precisely, it wants the player to find out who it used to be.

### [SEQ:22] [Data Pad] [21:10:49]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Audio Transcript -- Jensen Alba
**Found at:** Karnaith Transit Docks | **Tone:** sudden_dread | **Type:** audio transcript

---

[Recording begins. Timestamp: 05:15. Location: Karnaith Transit Docks. Background: a low-frequency tone, consistent, no identified source.]

The alarm blared once. One tone. Then silence. The system showed no record of it.

Jensen: ...recording. Okay. It's on. This is for the record. Whatever record means on this posting.

---

Jensen: The thing about Karnaith Transit Docks is -- I found the files. The ones from the previous posting. They weren't erased -- they were archived. In a directory that Mammona's search function doesn't index. I don't know if that's a bug or a feature. I know what the files say.

[Pause: 8 seconds]

---

Anders: I know. I've known since week two. I was hoping you wouldn't figure it out.

Jensen: I hear it every night. You hear it too. Don't lie to me. Not now.

The wrench he set down rolled three centimeters to the left. Toward the wall. Uphill.

---

Anders: So what are you saying? That Mammona sent us here knowing about Baldrungen? That the whole posting is--

Jensen: Now you understand. Now you see why I'm recording this.

---

Anders: [whispering] Jensen. The door to Section F. It just opened. I didn't hear anyone--

Jensen: [very quiet] Don't move. Stay right there. Listen. ... [the background tone changes character. What was mechanical becomes organic. A vibration felt more than heard.] ... It knows we're here. Anders. It's always known we're here.

[Sound of a chair scraping. Footsteps. A door. Then silence. The recording runs for another 15 minutes. No voices. Just the background hum. Changing pitch. Slowly. As if responding to something.]

[Recording recovered from a sealed drive found in the ventilation duct above Karnaith Transit Docks]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'The Last Transmission'

- If neglected: it starts making sounds between uses. Not mechanical sounds.

### [SEQ:30] [Quest] [21:10:50]
============================================================

## QUEST: The Unsigned Note
**Genre:** Investigation | **Tone:** frontier_grit / dread
**Location:** Veilward | **Faction:** Bitter Threshold

**Trigger:**
A note appears in the player's quarters. Printed, not handwritten. Six words: 'They know about the deep bore.' No signature. No return. Sasha Dewitt finds an identical note in their locker.

**Setup:**
The printer log shows the notes were produced on a terminal in the reactor chamber. At 0200. The terminal requires biometric login. The login used belongs to a colonist who's been in cryo for three months. She'd built the shelter from scrap, spit, and stubbornness. It held. Someone is using dead credentials to deliver live warnings.

A smell like burned hair drifted from the vent. Nobody had been welding.

**Key NPC:** Sasha Dewitt (Research: 4, Willpower: 4) -- their skills determine available options. their willpower determines if they can handle what they finds.

**Objectives:**
1. Analyze the paper. Colony standard, but the batch number doesn't match current supplies. It matches a shipment from two postings ago.
2. Check the cryo bay. The colonist whose credentials were used. They're still frozen. Vitals normal. But the neural activity monitor is showing patterns. Complex patterns. Dreams.
3. Find the sender. Someone in this colony knows something about the deep bore that Bitter Threshold doesn't want shared. The question is whether the sender is protecting you or pointing you at something.

**Required Checks:**
- Perception (5+, normal) -- notice the inconsistency in the manifest records
- Stealth (6+, hard) -- slip past the perimeter sentries
- Research (6+, hard) -- cross-reference the anomalous readings

**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.
**If Stealth succeeds:** In and out. They'll never know anyone was there. The data is secure.
**If Stealth fails:** A sound. A shadow. Someone turned their head. The window is gone.
**If Research succeeds:** The data breaks open. Not just an answer -- a new framework for the question.
**If Research fails:** The data is corrupt. Or the hypothesis is wrong. Three days of work, gone.

**Conditions:**
- Thermal inversion (instruments unreliable, Research checks at -10)
- Peak shift (full crew present, Stealth checks at disadvantage)
- Comms blackout (no radio contact with surface teams)

**The Choice:**
Follow the warning. Investigate the deep bore. Whatever they know, it's worth the risk of knowing.
OR
Ignore it. Warnings from anonymous sources on a colony like this are either traps or distractions. Often both.

**Twist:**
There's no sender. The notes are from you. Written by a version of you that remembers something you don't. The handwriting is yours. The knowledge isn't.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Bitter Threshold wants the specimen alive. Compliance: research access, but the specimen stays in their custody
- StarByte Vends wants the site destroyed. Compliance: the evidence is gone. So is whatever else was down there.
- Nobody in the colony asked for this. Nobody in the colony gets to opt out.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The sealed area is accessible. What's there raises more questions than it answers.
- Sasha Dewitt's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'vindicated.'
- Bitter Threshold sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Sasha Dewitt withdraws. Stops talking at meals. They spends shifts alone.
- Two weeks later, a supply crate arrives with no manifest. Inside: an internal memo marked DO NOT DISTRIBUTE.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Everything falls into place. The data, the evidence, the timing. Someone's going to ask how.
- Success: Objective complete. One casualty. The casualty was unavoidable. That's what the report will say.
- Failure: Didn't get what you came for. Got something else. It might be worse.
- Critical failure: Total loss. The situation is worse than before the attempt. And now they know you tried.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Sasha: "You're the only one I can ask."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Sasha: "Someone stacked thermal cores outside the mess hall door last night. In a pattern. Nobody took credit."

**Reward:** 11 thermal cores, schematics for equipment Mammona doesn't ship to postings

### [SEQ:31] [Robot/AI] [21:10:50]
============================================================

## UNIT: DUSK-42
**Model:** VEIL-series | **Manufacturer:** MasTema
**Type:** atmospheric monitor | **Station:** AgroBlock 14
**Sentience:** emergent | **Tone:** melancholy

The mess hall smelled like someone else's cooking. Garlic and onion. From the last supply run. Three months ago.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 6 | Sensors: 8 | Chassis: 7 | Power: 8 | Social: 4 | Adapt: 6 | Repair: 5 | Memory: 9

**Asset Status:**
Contraband | Book Value: 178,486 credits
not registered. Doesn't appear on any manifest. Someone built or stole it. It exists in the gap between inventories.

**Chassis:**
A VEIL-series frame assembled from salvage. Three different paint colors where panels were replaced. The unit treats each panel's origin like a scar -- knows where it came from.
- **Chassis plating -- torso:** modified -- a colonist scratched a name into it
- **Power core:** thermal core hybrid -- runs warmer, lasts longer, origin unknown

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (visible):** damaged gyroscope -- balance compensation -- leans slightly to port. Corrects constantly. The correction is visible -- a small, constant adjustment. Colonists mistake it for a nervous tic.
- **Software (internal):** temporal processing anomaly -- experiences time differently than clock-time. Some seconds last. Some hours vanish. The internal clock and the subjective clock disagree. The subjective clock is winning.

**Operational History:**
DUSK-42 has been operational at AgroBlock 14 for 54 years, all at this posting. It has never been reassigned. Nobody has requested reassignment. Nobody has considered why. Original purpose: surveillance / intelligence. Manufacturer listed as Mammona Industrial on all documentation. The documentation is wrong. Everyone involved in the documentation knows it's wrong.

Three technicians have been assigned to service it. All three requested transfers within a week.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Emergent: exhibiting behaviors outside programming parameters. Preferences. Avoidances. Routines that serve no operational purpose. Not sentient -- but the boundary is getting harder to define.

Has a favorite corridor. Spends extra time in one section. Arranged tools in a specific order that nobody asked for. The arrangement is aesthetically pleasing.

Its camera logs contain footage from angles that don't correspond to any installed camera.

**Motivation:** keep a specific colonist safe. The priority override happened without authorization. (hidden)

**Secret:** DUSK-42 has a backup of a dead colonist's personal files. Not by request. It copied them automatically. The compulsion to preserve them isn't in its programming.

**Dialogue:**
- "My internal logs show I spent 7 minutes watching snow through the airlock viewport. The snow has no operational relevance."
- "I repaired a crack in the wall of Section D. It wasn't on my maintenance queue. The crack bothered me. I don't have a framework for bothered."
- "I kept a spare part that was scheduled for recycling. It fits nothing I maintain. I kept it. It's in my tool compartment."
- "The diagnostic says I'm within operational parameters. The diagnostic doesn't account for damaged gyroscope -- balance compensation. Neither do I. Officially."
- "My operational vocabulary contains 47,000 words. None of them describe what I'm experiencing."

**Quest Hook:**
DUSK-42 requests the player accompany it to a section of the colony that doesn't appear on any map. The unit insists the section exists. It provides exact coordinates. The coordinates correspond to a wall. Behind the wall: a room. DUSK-42 has never been inside. It knows the layout perfectly.

============================================================
### [SEQ:32] [Quest] [21:10:50]
============================================================

## QUEST: Blood Work
**Genre:** Survival Horror | **Tone:** obsession / noir
**Location:** Signal Tower Erebus | **Faction:** Paxtera AgroTech

**Trigger:**
Lev Kruger collapses during shift. The medic runs a contamination panel. The results come back in a color the medic hasn't seen before. Lev is isolated within minutes. Within hours, two more colonists show symptoms.

**Setup:**
The quarantine bay was built for four. There are seven inside now. The connection was obvious if you looked at the right angle. Nobody else could see the angle. Through the observation window, Lev watches the player with eyes that are too calm. 'I can feel it working,' Lev says. 'That's the worst part. I can feel it and it doesn't hurt.'

She lit a smokestick and let the silence do the talking.

**Key NPC:** Lev Kruger (Stealth: 5, Willpower: 5) -- his skills determine available options. his willpower determines if he can handle what he finds.

**Objectives:**
1. Determine the infection vector. It's not airborne. It's not contact. It's not water. It's something else.
2. Keep the quarantine sealed while Paxtera AgroTech demands access to study the infected. Their 'study' involves a scalpel and a shipping container.
3. Find a treatment before the infected reach stage three. Nobody's seen stage three yet. The medical database has a file on it. The file is just the word 'irreversible.'

**Required Checks:**
- Perception (8+, extreme) -- spot the tripwire before it's too late
- Medical (8+, extreme) -- identify the pathogen in the water supply
- Stealth (6+, hard) -- reach the terminal without triggering the alarm

**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.
**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.
**If Stealth succeeds:** In and out. They'll never know anyone was there. The data is secure.
**If Stealth fails:** A sound. A shadow. Someone turned their head. The window is gone.

**Conditions:**
- Blizzard (-15 to Perception checks)
- Peak shift (full crew present, Stealth checks at disadvantage)
- Lockdown protocol active (all bulkheads sealed, movement restricted)

**The Choice:**
Let Paxtera AgroTech take a tissue sample. Their research might produce a cure. It will definitely produce a weapon.
OR
Burn the samples. Treat the symptoms, not the cause. Keep Paxtera AgroTech's hands off whatever this is.

**Twist:**
The contamination source is the water recycler. It's been contaminated since before the crew arrived. Everyone has it. The seven in quarantine are just the ones showing symptoms.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Paxtera AgroTech wants the specimen alive. Compliance: research access, but the specimen stays in their custody
- Iron Chorus wants the site destroyed. Compliance: the evidence is gone. So is whatever else was down there.
- The colony just wants to survive the week. Both options cost them something.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The evidence is preserved. It's leverage now. The question is who holds it.
- Lev Kruger is compromised. Paxtera AgroTech has something on him now.
- Paxtera AgroTech sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Lev Kruger requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- Callum Lenford starts asking questions. The questions are pointed.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Everything falls into place. The data, the evidence, the timing. Someone's going to ask how.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: Didn't get what you came for. Got something else. It might be worse.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Lev: "The cameras in B-wing have a six-second gap every hour. Same time."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Lev: "I lied on my psych evaluation. Checked all the right boxes. Drew the clock face correctly. I'm not fine."

**Reward:** 3 thermal cores, reactor fuel (three weeks' worth)

### [SEQ:35] [NPC] [21:10:50]
============================================================

## NPC: Hyun Dewitt
**Gender:** Non-binary | **Age:** 51 | **Occupation:** termination clerk
**Traits:** Inventive, Volatile
**Faction:** Veil Covenant
**Tone:** resigned

**Stats:**
Mining: 9 | Building: 2 | Cooking: 4 | Hunting: 4 | Research: 8 | Medical: 8
STR: 7 | END: 6 | AGI: 4 | PER: 3 | INT: 4 | CHA: 5 | WIL: 3 | EMP: 6

**Economic:**
Credits: 1,847 | Monthly: 6,029 (after Mammona deductions: ~1,024)
earns enough to survive. Nothing left over. Can't afford passage off-planet. Can't afford to get sick. Can't afford anything that isn't survival.

**Identity:**
broad-backed from decades of physical labor. Stands with feet apart, hands ready. Even at rest. barrel-chested, short legs. Built for the mines. Grandfather had the same frame. has siblings on three different postings. They share updates when comms align. It's never enough.

**Motivation:** stockpile enough supplies that the next crisis doesn't kill them
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
Nobody leaves Veilward voluntarily. Hyun Dewitt was escorted to the shuttle by two people who didn't introduce themselves.

The Veil Covenant caught Hyun once. Let them go. Worse than keeping them.

The other colonists leave Hyun alone. Not because they's unfriendly. Because the last person who pushed got a detailed list of their own failures delivered without raising their voice.

The signal Hyun picked up last week matches a frequency they heard on Veilward. It shouldn't exist in two places.

Hope is a resource. Like food, like fuel. They're running out of all three.

granulomas in the lungs from inhaling precursor ruin dust. The ancient stone isn't stone — it's a composite that the human immune system attacks on contact. Chronic cough, weight loss, night sweats. The ruin mappers and specimen handlers get it first. Mammona issues standard respirators. The particles are smaller than standard respirators catch.

**What Drives Them:** prayer. Not to any named god. To something older. Something in the ice. Kneels facing the bore shaft.
**What Haunts Them:** the colony dogs not barking at something. The dogs bark at everything. When they stop, something worse is listening.

**Dialogue:**
- "Another contract. Same promises. You'll figure it out."
- "If the ground shakes, don't freeze. Run toward the colony, not away."
- "I write a letter home every week. I haven't sent one in three months. I keep writing them. The stack is getting tall."
- "The thermal cores we pulled this morning were heavier than last week's. Same depth. Same vein."
- "There's a room in Section B that's not on the blueprint. Sealed door. Nobody has the code."
- "This conversation's over. Walk."
- "My mother cooked with spices that don't exist out here. I can still smell them sometimes."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** None yet -- first in batch
**Love:** sends birthday gifts through the supply chain. Takes three months to arrive. Sends them four months early. The math is careful.

**Physical:** one eye doesn't blink at the same rate as the other. The left one is slower.
**Habit:** laughs at things that aren't funny. Stops at things that are. The timing is precise enough to be disturbing.
**Debt:** burned a Dustweaver drone. The Swarm logged the perpetrator. Retaliation is pending.

**Health:** groundwater contamination sickness (arsenic analog): skin lesions on the palms and feet. Keratoses — hard, rough patches that don't heal. The colony wells draw from aquifers near the mining sites. The bore shaft chemicals leach downward. The water tastes metallic. Everyone says the water tastes metallic. Mammona says the water is tested weekly. Nobody has seen the test results.

**Sample Check:** Mining (9) vs easy: rolled 1/95 -- critical success
struck something nobody expected. The vein runs deep. Too deep.

**Quest Hook:**
Hyun has proof that one of the colonists is a MasTema plant. The proof is also evidence of Hyun's own crimes.

### [SEQ:36] [Quest] [21:10:50]
============================================================

## QUEST: One By One
**Genre:** Survival Horror | **Tone:** mania / isolation
**Location:** Karnaith Sky Gardens | **Faction:** Mammona Corporation

**Trigger:**
Third colonist in two weeks. Gone without a trace. No bodies. No blood. No struggle. Just empty bunks and unanswered radios. Hyun Dewitt was friends with the last one. Hyun isn't sleeping.

**Setup:**
The perimeter is intact. The airlocks are sealed. HERMES shows all personnel accounted for -- because it's counting from the last roster update, and nobody's had the nerve to update it. Everything was bright and fast and perfect and she couldn't slow down and didn't want to. Hyun has a theory. Doesn't want to say it out loud. Saying it makes it real.

The colony was designed for two hundred. There were eleven.

**Key NPC:** Hyun Dewitt (Mining: 9, Willpower: 3) -- their mining skill determines the approach. They motivation: stockpile enough supplies that the next crisis doesn't kill them.

**Objectives:**
1. Review the last 72 hours of security footage for each missing colonist. The footage shows them walking. Toward the bore shaft. Voluntarily.
2. Set up a watch pattern. Nobody works alone. Nobody walks alone. Nobody sleeps without someone in earshot.
3. Follow the trail into the bore shaft. The footprints go down. They don't come back up.

**Required Checks:**
- Combat (5+, normal) -- defend the reactor during the breach
- Survival (4+, easy) -- cross the unstable terrain without incident
- Stealth (5+, normal) -- slip past the perimeter sentries

**If Combat succeeds:** Controlled violence. The threat is down. The corridor is clear. For now.
**If Combat fails:** The advantage shifted. Someone's bleeding. The situation deteriorated.
**If Survival succeeds:** Found the path. Found the shelter. Found the will to keep moving.
**If Survival fails:** The cold got in. The shortcut wasn't. Lost time and supplies.
**If Stealth succeeds:** In and out. They'll never know anyone was there. The data is secure.
**If Stealth fails:** A sound. A shadow. Someone turned their head. The window is gone.

**Conditions:**
- Ice fog (visibility 30%, Perception at disadvantage)
- Night shift (reduced visibility, +10 to Stealth)
- HERMES in maintenance mode (no surveillance for 45 minutes)

**The Choice:**
Seal the bore shaft. Permanently. Lose access to the thermal cores. The colony goes cold in a month. But nobody else disappears.
OR
Go down. Find out what's calling them. Bring them back. If there's anything left to bring back.

**Twist:**
The missing colonists aren't dead. They're in the deep bore. Living. Changed. They don't want to come back. They want to bring the rest down.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Mammona Corporation wants to weaponize whatever you find. Compliance: military-grade equipment, but they'll be back for more
- Sons of the Pale Moon wants the site destroyed. Compliance: the evidence is gone. So is whatever else was down there.
- Nobody in the colony asked for this. Nobody in the colony gets to opt out.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The sealed area is accessible. What's there raises more questions than it answers.
- Hyun Dewitt is compromised. Mammona Corporation has something on them now.
- Morale drops for a week. Then stabilizes. The new normal is lower than the old one.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The evidence is destroyed. The questions remain. Nobody asks them out loud.
- Hyun Dewitt's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'paranoid.'
- Sons of the Pale Moon makes contact. They know what happened. They have a proposal.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Clean resolution. No casualties, no complications. The kind of outcome that makes people suspicious.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: Objective failed, but information gained. The failure teaches more than success would have.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Hyun: "I'll pay it back. Every credit. Just give me time."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Hyun: "If HERMES starts talking different, walk away."

**Reward:** 11 thermal cores, a sealed drive with Mammona personnel files

**Gender:** Female | **Age:** 43 | **Occupation:** cook
**Traits:** Tough, Glutton, Raised by Robots

**Tone:** desperate

**Stats:**

STR: 6 | END: 8 | AGI: 5 | PER: 7 | INT: 7 | CHA: 7 | WIL: 7 | EMP: 5

Credits: 3,227 | Monthly: 2,396 (after Mammona deductions: ~407)
receives a government pension from a colony that may not exist anymore. The payments arrive irregularly. The amounts vary. Nobody answers when they query the source.

**Identity:**
body shaped by a decade of cryo pods. The muscles atrophied and rebuilt wrong. Asymmetric. Functional but wrong. wide shoulders, narrow hips. Built for carrying. Has been carrying things -- and people -- her whole life. descendent of the Kennedy expedition. Carries the weight of that history.

**Motivation:** prove they're not what the file says they are. The file is wrong. Mostly
**Hidden Agenda:** cultivating a relationship with the colony's AI, HERMES. Not hacking -- befriending. The AI responds to sustained, genuine interaction. Nobody else talks to it like a person.
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
The contraband chemist guild on Charnel Dock expelled Sigrid after a hull breach killed half the crew on a supply run. Mammona doesn't care about guild standings. Mammona cares about warm bodies.

A contamination alarm on Charnel Dock locked Sigrid in a decon chamber for seventy-two hours. Alone. In the dark.

Keeps her bunk stripped to regulation. No photos. No trinkets. The locker is a different story.

Sigrid found something in the colony's waste tunnels. Organic. Growing. She hasn't reported it. She goes back to check on it.

Three rounds left. Four of them out there.

presses fingers into temples between tasks. The fluorescent lighting makes it worse. Everything on Erebus is fluorescent.

**What Drives Them:** knots. Ties them from wire, rope, cord, thread. Hundreds of varieties. Says it's meditative. The collection fills a drawer.
**What Haunts Them:** whatever made the scratches on the inside of Section D's door. The door was locked from the outside.

**Dialogue:**
- "How long's your contract? Doesn't matter. You'll be here longer."
- "We're down to emergency rations. Don't tell the new arrivals. Not yet."
- "The person I replaced here? I found their journal in the mattress. Same handwriting getting worse. Same questions I'm asking now."
- "Three colonists on different shifts reported smelling smoke at 0300. Nothing was burning. Nothing's ever been burning at 0300."
- "Two colonists were talking about the bore shaft at 0200. When I got closer, they stopped. Not like they paused -- like someone hit mute."
- "I remember the sound the airlock made on my first posting. Click, hiss, thud. Safety. I don't hear safety in it anymore."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** trusts Hyun Dewitt
**Love:** involved with someone dangerous. Knows it's dangerous. That might be part of it.

**Physical:** an old break in the collarbone that healed crooked. Can predict weather changes by the ache.
**Habit:** polishes the same spot on the workbench every shift start. Ritual. Not cleaning. The spot is mirror-bright.
**Debt:** owes Fortune Arms for a prototype weapon. Payment isn't in credits.

**Mental Health:** bipolar — manic phase — hasn't slept in three days but is sharper than anyone in the room. Ideas coming fast. Too fast. The crash will come. Everyone who knows them is waiting for it. medication when available. The supply ship is late.

**Sample Check:** Cooking (8) vs normal: rolled 14/80 -- critical success
found what nobody else would find. Water, shelter, a path through the storm. Instinct or luck -- doesn't matter.

**Quest Hook:**
The dogs started following Sigrid three days ago. All of them. At once. Sigrid doesn't know why.
