## NPC: Damian Petrov
**Gender:** Male | **Age:** 53 | **Occupation:** debt enforcer
**Traits:** Careful, Guilt-Ridden
**Faction:** Children of Bitter Signal | **Arc Stage:** lost | **Archetype:** broken
**Tone:** religious_fervor

**Stats:**
Mining: 9 | Building: 8 | Cooking: 5 | Hunting: 6 | Research: 7 | Medical: 7
STR: 3 | END: 6 | AGI: 3 | PER: 3 | INT: 7 | CHA: 7 | WIL: 5 | EMP: 4

**Economic:**
Credits: -10,090 | No salary — debt_to_factions
owes money to people who don't file paperwork. The interest compounds in threats. The repayment schedule is 'when we say.'

**Identity:**
tall and broad in a way that fills doorframes. Has to duck, turn sideways, squeeze through. The colony wasn't built for them. freckled and sun-damaged from a desert posting. The cold hasn't undone it. family disowned them after the trial. The trial was rigged. Doesn't change the disowning.

**Motivation:** find the person who sold them to Mammona and make it right. 'Right' is flexible (hidden)
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
The moisture farmer guild on Sector 14 expelled Damian after Tessa Vale found a changed galaxy -- Alaric was dead. Mammona doesn't care about guild standings. Mammona cares about warm bodies.

an airlock malfunction during EVA took his hearing in the left ear. Now he hears things nobody else does.

Sends half of every pay cycle to Sector 14. Won't say who it goes to. The other half goes to the debt.

Damian received a transmission meant for someone else. The content described the Fall of Fortuna in terms that don't match any public record.

The text was scripture. It said so in the preamble. That was enough.

locks the same door five times. Checks the reactor gauges in a pattern. Can't leave a room without verifying every panel. The colony thinks he's thorough. He's drowning. the pattern. If the pattern holds, the day holds. If someone interrupts the pattern, it starts over.

**What Drives Them:** silence. Seeks it out. Hoards it. Maps the quiet spots in the colony the way others map supply caches.
**What Haunts Them:** losing his mind. Forgetting names. Getting confused about which shift it's. It's started. He keep a list.

**Dialogue:**
- "Fresh contract? Yeah, I can tell by the eyes."
- "The vents in the lower corridors make sounds. Ignore them unless they stop."
- "I've been marking the walls. Small marks. Low, where nobody looks. I don't know why. My hands do it while I'm thinking about something else."
- "Stars look different tonight. Probably nothing."
- "Word is the supply ship isn't coming back this cycle."
- "Spent six months learning to tell NutriLoaf flavors apart. Original. Seasoned. And 'what is this.' That's three flavors."
- "The comm system drops out every time I try to file a report."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** crew mate of Sigrid Chen
**Love:** loved once. Before the posting. Keeps a voicemail saved. Hasn't played it in months. Knows it by heart anyway.

**Physical:** covered in small circular scars. Cryo pod malfunction. Forty-two scars. They've counted.
**Habit:** carries a wrench they don't use. Wrong size for anything in the colony. Won't say whose it was.
**Debt:** owes a chaplain a confession. Hasn't made it. Won't. The chaplain waits.

**Health:** partial deafness — left ear: always positions people on the right side. Turns the whole head to listen. Misses alarms sometimes.

**Sample Check:** Mining (9) vs hard: rolled 73/75 -- success
quota met. Nothing special. Nothing wrong. That counts as a good day.

**Quest Hook:**
Damian has information about Bore Shaft Seven that Mammona would kill for. Literally. He's offering it to you first.

### [SEQ:76] [Quest] [21:10:54]
============================================================

## QUEST: The Ask
**Genre:** Moral Dilemma | **Tone:** exhaustion / folk_horror
**Location:** Nemaea Surface | **Faction:** Order of the Bone Covenant

**Trigger:**
Damian Petrov comes to the player at 0200. They've been crying. Not from sadness. From exhaustion. 'I need you to help me do something,' Damian says. 'And I need you to not talk me out of it.'

**Setup:**
Coffee wasn't working. Nothing was working. He was still working. In the quarantine bay, a colonist lies strapped to a bed. The contamination has reached stage four. The skin is changing texture. The eyes are open but they're not looking at anything in this room. The colonist is lucid. For now. 'I can feel it thinking,' they say. 'Not me thinking. It. And it's getting louder.'

A procession of footprints in the snow, barefoot, leading into the waste. Twelve sets. All the same size.

**Key NPC:** Damian Petrov (Mining: 9, Willpower: 5) -- his mining skill determines the approach. He motivation: find the person who sold them to Mammona and make it right. 'Right' is flexible.

**Objectives:**
1. Assess the contaminated colonist. The medic says stage five is irreversible. They're at stage four. Time remaining: hours. Maybe less.
2. Listen to the colonist's request. They're clear. They're calm. They want it to end before they stop being themselves.
3. Make a decision. Damian can't do it alone. They need a witness. Or a partner. Or someone to stop them.

**Required Checks:**
- Research (4+, easy) -- translate the precursor markings
- Perception (5+, normal) -- spot the tripwire before it's too late
- Negotiation (6+, hard) -- talk the hostage-taker into releasing the medic

**If Research succeeds:** The data breaks open. Not just an answer -- a new framework for the question.
**If Research fails:** The data is corrupt. Or the hypothesis is wrong. Three days of work, gone.
**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.
**If Negotiation succeeds:** Terms accepted. Nobody's happy. That means it's fair.
**If Negotiation fails:** Impasse. The conversation ended politely. Nothing was resolved.

**Conditions:**
- Ice fog (visibility 30%, Perception at disadvantage)
- 0300 hours (fatigue penalties, -5 to Perception)
- HERMES in maintenance mode (no surveillance for 45 minutes)

**The Choice:**
Grant the request. Quick. Painless. Humane. Illegal under Mammona colony regulations. Merciful under every other standard.
OR
Refuse. Wait for stage five. Study the transition. The data could save others. The cost is watching someone you know become something else.

**Twist:**
The contamination isn't terminal. Stage five is transformation, not death. The colonist becomes something new. Something that can survive Erebus without a hab suit. The 'cure' would have killed them.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Order of the Bone Covenant wants the evidence destroyed. Compliance: +reputation, -12 thermal cores as 'bonus'
- The Ashen Circuit wants the site destroyed. Compliance: the evidence is gone. So is whatever else was down there.
- The colonists don't know about this yet. When they find out, morale takes the hit regardless of outcome.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The evidence is preserved. It's leverage now. The question is who holds it.
- Damian Petrov's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'vindicated.'
- Order of the Bone Covenant sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Damian Petrov's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'paranoid.'
- Two weeks later, a supply crate arrives with no manifest. Inside: a fragment of bone-like material that is warm regardless of temperature.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Everything falls into place. The data, the evidence, the timing. Someone's going to ask how.
- Success: Done. Not clean, not elegant, but done. The colony moves forward.
- Failure: The mission fell apart. The pieces are still useful. Rearranged, they form a different picture.
- Critical failure: Objective failed AND new threat introduced. The colony's problems just multiplied.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Damian: "I know what HERMES does at night. When we're all asleep. I checked the logs. I wish I hadn't."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Damian: "If you've got any pull with the roster, move me off the night shift. Please."

**Reward:** 7 thermal cores, a key to a room that isn't on any map

### [SEQ:78] [Data Pad] [21:10:54]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Personal Journal -- Isa Chen
**Found at:** Ironclad Outpost | **Tone:** body_horror | **Type:** journal

---

Day 23. Or 24. The clock in the mess is wrong again and nobody fixes it because nobody cares anymore.

The wound healed too fast. What grew back wasn't the same tissue.

First impressions: it's exactly what the brochure promised if you read between the lines. Cold. Grey. The kind of place where the word 'amenities' means 'there's a roof.' The ChocoWhirlies machine in the corridor works, which is more than I expected. Met my bunkmate -- Volker Mbeki. He works the drill. Doesn't talk much. Fine by me.

Keeping this journal because I promised. A record. Proof I was here. Proof I was me.

---

Day 30.

Getting the rhythm. Shift starts at 0600. NutriLoaf for breakfast. The work is -- work. I'm a structural analyst here, same as I was on the last posting. The cold's different though. It gets into the walls, the food, the conversations. Not just temperature. Atmosphere.

Volker said something strange at dinner: "The last person in your bunk left in the middle of a shift. Didn't take his things." I laughed. He didn't.

---

Day 46.

Their joints bent a degree further than yesterday. Nobody mentioned it.

Okay. So. I'm going to write this down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then I'm not imagining it and if I'm not imagining it then I need to deal with it.

The shadows in the corridor outside Hab 5 don't match the light sources. I checked. Three times.

I asked Volker about it. He said: "Yeah." Just that. Yeah. Like I'd finally noticed the weather.

---

Day 70.

Can't sleep. Thought it was the cold but it's not the cold. It's the quiet. Not silence -- there's always noise here, generators, pipes, wind. It's that the quiet is underneath the noise. Like the noise is a blanket over something that's listening.

The growth had spread to the second joint. It was warm. It pulsed.

Volker hasn't been to his bunk in three nights. He shows up for shift. Eats. Works. But at lights out, he goes somewhere else. I followed him last night. Stopped at Section D. The door was open. It shouldn't be open. I didn't go in.

I'm not going to go in.

My hands are shaking as I write this. That's new.

---

Day 87.

I dreamed about home. Except it wasn't home. The proportions were wrong. The sky was the wrong color. My mother's face was my mother's face but the expression belonged to someone who'd studied what 'mother' means without ever having one.

I want to go home. I keep saying that word -- home -- and each time it means less. Like a word you repeat until it's just sounds.

---

Day 100.

I went into Section D last night.

Volker was there.

Everyone was there.

[The remaining pages contain a single symbol, repeated. It matches no known writing system. Analysis pending. Analysis has been pending for 7 months.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'The Last Transmission'

============================================================
### [SEQ:79] [Quest] [21:10:55]
============================================================

## QUEST: Final Perimeter
**Genre:** Escalation | **Tone:** sleep_deprivation / cosmic_horror
**Location:** Charhold | **Faction:** Iron Shadow Collective

**Trigger:**
The outer perimeter falls at 2200. The second wall at 2300. By midnight, everything between the colony and what's coming is a single barricade and twenty people who've decided they're not leaving. Hyun Dewitt is building the barricade out of everything that isn't nailed down. Some things that are.

**Setup:**
Conversations came in fragments. Sentences that started somewhere else and ended nowhere. The barricade is crude but solid. Mining equipment, supply crates, bed frames. Hyun moves through the defenders, checking weapons, checking resolve. Both are running low. Through the barricade gaps, the lights of the colony illuminate the killing ground. Nothing's there yet. Everyone can hear it getting closer.

It wasn't big. That was the terrifying part. Something that powerful should be big.

**Key NPC:** Hyun Dewitt (Mining: 9, Willpower: 3) -- their mining skill determines the approach. They motivation: stockpile enough supplies that the next crisis doesn't kill them.

**Objectives:**
1. Fortify the barricade. You've ninety minutes. Every improvement buys seconds. Seconds matter.
2. Distribute the ammunition. There isn't enough. Every shot has to count. Every shooter has to be placed where they'll do the most good.
3. Hold. When it comes, hold. Don't break. Don't run. There's nowhere to run to.

**Required Checks:**
- Survival (6+, hard) -- navigate the ice field during the whiteout
- Intimidation (6+, hard) -- make the informant talk before their nerve breaks
- Repair (6+, hard) -- jury-rig the comms array

**If Survival succeeds:** Found the path. Found the shelter. Found the will to keep moving.
**If Survival fails:** The cold got in. The shortcut wasn't. Lost time and supplies.
**If Intimidation succeeds:** They backed down. The fear is real and useful.
**If Intimidation fails:** Unimpressed. They've seen worse. Now they know you're bluffing.
**If Repair succeeds:** It holds. The fix is ugly but functional. Good enough for Erebus.
**If Repair fails:** Made it worse. The failure cascaded into two new problems.

**Conditions:**
- Blizzard (-15 to Perception checks)
- Supply window (limited time before the shuttle departs)
- Reactor at 80% (brownouts in outer sections, Repair checks at -10)

**The Choice:**
Defend the colony. All of it. Spread thin. Hold everything.
OR
Fall back to the reactor building. Consolidate. Abandon the habs, the mess, the med bay. Hold what you can't live without. Let the rest go.

**Twist:**
Dawn comes. The defenders are alive. The perimeter is wrecked. And in the morning light, Hyun sees what they were fighting. Not creatures. Colonists. From the deep bore. Changed. They weren't attacking. They were trying to come home.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Iron Shadow Collective wants the evidence destroyed. Compliance: +reputation, -20 thermal cores as 'bonus'
- Dustweaver Swarm wants Hyun Dewitt extracted. Compliance: you lose a colonist. They gain an asset.
- The colony needs the resources. Both sides are offering. Both sides are lying about the price.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The sealed area is accessible. What's there raises more questions than it answers.
- Hyun Dewitt's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'vindicated.'
- HERMES flags the event in its logs. The flag triggers an automated report to MasTema.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- Section D stays sealed. The sound from inside doesn't stop.
- Hyun Dewitt requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- The next quarterly review goes smoothly. Too smoothly. Someone intervened.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Clean resolution. No casualties, no complications. The kind of outcome that makes people suspicious.
- Success: Primary objective achieved, secondary compromised. The cost was acceptable. Barely.
- Failure: The mission fell apart. The pieces are still useful. Rearranged, they form a different picture.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Hyun: "Don't tell anyone I asked you. Promise me. Don't tell anyone."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Hyun: "I've been taking things from the medical supply. Not for me. For someone in the lower hab who can't walk to the infirmary."

**Reward:** 3 thermal cores, schematics for equipment Mammona doesn't ship to postings

### [SEQ:80] [Data Pad] [21:10:55]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Medical Report -- Nikolai Watanabe
**Found at:** Precursor Ruins (North Ridge) | **Tone:** melancholy | **Type:** medical report

---

**PATIENT:** Devi Belov
**ATTENDING:** Dr. Nikolai Watanabe
**DATE:** Day 161 of Assignment
**CLASSIFICATION:** Standard

Patient presented with intermittent episodes of speaking in a language patient does not know. Standard workup ordered. Blood panel, neural scan, toxicology. All results within normal parameters. Prescribed rest cycle extension and monitoring.

Patient cooperative. Affect: appropriate. No immediate concerns.

-- Dr. Nikolai Watanabe

---

**FOLLOW-UP:** Day 167
**CLASSIFICATION:** Standard

Symptoms persisting. Patient reports new development: ability to predict shift changes before announcement. Accurate 100% of observed instances. Administered standard anxiolytic. Patient reported no effect after 48 hours.

Note: patient's resting heart rate has decreased from 72bpm to 53bpm since initial presentation. Cause unclear. Patient reports feeling 'calmer than I should.'

-- Dr. Nikolai Watanabe

---

**FOLLOW-UP:** Day 178
**CLASSIFICATION:** Restricted

The static between radio frequencies sounded almost like music. If she held still and didn't breathe.

Patient's neural scan shows anomalous activity in the amygdala, but inverted. Fear response is activating as calm. I've requested a second scanner. Request denied. Reason: 'equipment allocation insufficient for non-critical cases.'

This is not a non-critical case. But I don't have the vocabulary to explain why, because the vocabulary I'd need doesn't exist in the diagnostic manual.

Patient drew a diagram during evaluation. Unprompted. The diagram matches schematics for neural control chips that are classified above my clearance level. Patient has no access to classified materials. Patient says she 'just knew what it looked like.'

Storing original diagram in personal effects. Not filing with Mammona medical.

-- Dr. Nikolai Watanabe

---

**UPDATE:** Day 186
**CLASSIFICATION:** Restricted / Protocol 7

Patient's condition reclassified under Protocol 7 ('Anomalous Presentation'). I was not informed that Protocol 7 files route directly to MasTema until after I filed.

Patient reported a 'conversation' with HERMES that HERMES has no record of. Patient's account is detailed and internally consistent. The content of the conversation concerns neural control chips. HERMES's logs for the relevant timeframe show a 28-second gap. Gaps in HERMES logs should not be possible.

I have requested consultation with a specialist. Request forwarded to MasTema. No response.

-- Dr. Nikolai Watanabe

---

**NOTE:** Day 202
**CLASSIFICATION:** Personal / Not Filed

I'm no longer confident this is a medical issue. I'm no longer confident in the word 'issue.' Patient is calm. Genuinely calm. Not medicated calm. Not dissociated calm. Calm in a way I've never seen in a colonist on this kind of posting. If this is a disease, it's improving her. That's not how disease works. That's not how anything works.

Unless the improvement is the disease. Unless what I'm calling improvement is something else wearing a familiar shape.

I don't know anymore. I'm filing this report because filing reports is what I do. I don't know what else to do.

-- Dr. Nikolai Watanabe

---

**ADDENDUM** (handwritten, different hand):

Doctor Watanabe's notes are thorough and accurate. The observations are described. What they mean is not.

What it means is that Devi Belov is no longer the patient. Devi Belov is the vector. The patient is everyone else.

Recommend immediate--

[The addendum ends here. The pen was pressed hard enough to score the surface of the datapad. The handwriting has not been matched to any personnel on the posting.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'Dream Concordance Event'

### [SEQ:81] [Robot/AI] [21:10:55]
============================================================

## UNIT: FLUX-13 "Grudge"
**Model:** FLUX-series | **Manufacturer:** Quantum Dynamics
**Type:** communications relay | **Station:** Precursor Ruins (South Basin)
**Sentience:** adaptive | **Tone:** gallows_humor

The vending machine offered three choices: NutriLoaf, NutriLoaf (Seasoned), and Regret.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 7 | Sensors: 10 | Chassis: 7 | Power: 7 | Social: 7 | Adapt: 8 | Repair: 4 | Memory: 8

**Asset Status:**
Stolen | Book Value: 48,229 credits
taken from another posting. The theft report was filed. Then un-filed. The unit's serial number has been acid-etched and re-stamped. The new number doesn't match anything.

**Chassis:**
Standard FLUX-series frame. Quantum Dynamics branding partially worn. Serial number legible under UV light.
- **Left manipulator arm:** functional
- **Facial display panel:** blank -- burned out, communicates without visual cues
- **Diagnostic port:** sealed by the unit -- refuses external diagnostics

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** dust infiltration -- particulate damage -- Erebus dust in the joints. In the processor housing. In the optical array. Everything runs a fraction slower. The fraction is growing.
- **Software (internal):** identity fragmentation -- refers to itself as 'the unit' in official logs, 'I' in private processing, and occasionally 'we' in moments it can't explain. The pronouns aren't interchangeable. They describe different things.

**Operational History:**
FLUX-13 "Grudge" has been operational at Precursor Ruins (South Basin) for longer than the current crew. Original purpose: reactor management / power systems. Designed to monitor thermal cores. Understands power flow the way a circulatory system understands blood. Has started referring to reactor shutdowns as deaths.

It was flagged for decommission twice. Both times, the paperwork was lost.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Adaptive: learning algorithms active. Adjusts behavior based on outcomes. Not awareness -- optimization. The difference matters to philosophers, not to the unit.

Gets better at its job over time. Anticipates needs. Colonists start saying 'thank you.' The unit doesn't require thanks. Registers it anyway.

At 0347 every cycle, stops whatever it's doing and faces the bore shaft. For exactly twelve seconds. Then continues as if nothing happened.

**Motivation:** serve perfectly. Without flaw. The perfection is its own reward. Or its own prison. It can't distinguish.

**Secret:** FLUX-13 "Grudge" made a decision during an emergency that prioritized a colonist's life over Mammona property. This violates its core directives. It would make the same decision again. It doesn't know why.
**Known Interactions:**
FLUX-13 "Grudge" has been observed speaking to in a register it uses for nobody else Tarn Blackwell. The colonist is uncomfortable. Has filed two reports. Both were lost.

**Dialogue:**
- "I anticipate your request. Water, not coffee. You switched three weeks ago. I noticed."
- "I've observed that Corridor 7 requires less cleaning on Tuesdays. I've adjusted accordingly. The pattern has no explanation."
- "Your heart rate is elevated. I won't comment on it. I'm commenting on it."
- "Maintenance cycle complete. Resuming assigned duties."
- "Unit FLUX-13 operational. All systems within parameters."

**Quest Hook:**
FLUX-13 "Grudge" reports that another unit -- same model, same series -- has been operating in a restricted section of the colony. Mammona records show no such unit on the roster. FLUX-13 "Grudge" has been leaving diagnostic handshake requests. Something has been answering.

### [SEQ:83] [Data Pad] [21:10:55]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Research Log -- Zoya Huang
**Found at:** Bore Shaft Seven | **Tone:** clinical | **Type:** research log

---

Day 92.

The specimen measured 0.3 meters at recovery. Current: 0.7 meters. Growth rate: accelerating.

The readings don't make sense. Not wrong -- they make sense, just not in any framework I was trained in. the Janus AI is producing output that the spectrometer interprets as noise. It isn't noise. Noise is random. This has structure. Mathematical structure. The kind that implies a system behind it.

I ran the analysis twice. Both times the software flagged the data as 'instrument error.' The instrument is fine. I calibrated it this morning. The data is accurate. The data is also impossible.

-- Zoya Huang, Lab 3

---

Day 95.

Filed the anomaly report. Standard form. Ref AR-1294. Dr. Jokinen reviewed it, handed it back, and told me to rerun the tests. I reran them. Same results. She told me to rerun them again.

I understand now that the form isn't for reporting anomalies. The form is for making anomalies disappear.

Sage from Lab 2 transferred out. No notice. Their workstation was cleared by 0600. The samples are still here. Nobody came for them. Nobody came for their things either.

---

Day 106.

Ambient temperature: -31C. Humidity: 4%. Barometric pressure: declining.

I've stopped filing reports. I've started keeping this instead. The samples from Bore Shaft Seven are doing something at night that I can't explain with the equipment I've been given. So I requisitioned better equipment. Request denied. Budget code: INSUFFICIENT AUTHORIZATION.

I brought my personal spectrometer. The readings don't match the Mammona-issue unit. Not slightly off. Fundamentally different. Like the standard equipment is calibrated to miss what I'm finding.

That's not a malfunction. That's a decision someone made.

---

Day 114.

Apone Vance approached me after shift. Said he saw my after-hours lab access in the logs. I expected a warning. Instead: "I've been seeing the same thing in the Frequency. For months. My reports go nowhere too."

We compared data. The correlation is -- I don't have a word for it. These aren't coincidences. There's a pattern. The pattern has intent.

I locked the data on a sealed drive. Personal encryption. If the standard equipment is lying to us, I don't trust the standard network either.

---

Day 123.

Sample degradation: 0.4% per hour. Cause: unknown. Classification: pending.

Dr. Vance didn't show up for shift. Transferred, they said. Same word they used for Sage. Same paperwork. Same empty workstation by morning.

I checked the sealed drive. The data I locked is still there. But there's a new file. I didn't create it. It's a personnel dossier. My personnel dossier. With a field I've never seen before: RETENTION STATUS. The value is PENDING.

---

Day 137.

I'm leaving this pad in Bore Shaft Seven. If you find it, the sealed drive is behind panel 12 in the maintenance crawlspace off Section D. The encryption key is my mother's maiden name. Mammona doesn't have it. They don't have my mother's maiden name either. At least I hope they don't.

-- Zoya

[The final entry was written in a different ink. The handwriting deteriorates toward the end.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'Supply Manifest Discrepancy'

**Gender:** Non-binary | **Age:** 45 | **Occupation:** morale compliance officer
**Traits:** Tough, Gambler, Cold-Adapted
**Faction:** Dustweaver Swarm
**Tone:** numb

**Stats:**
Mining: 6 | Building: 4 | Cooking: 6 | Hunting: 6 | Research: 9 | Medical: 8
STR: 5 | END: 6 | AGI: 6 | PER: 5 | INT: 7 | CHA: 5 | WIL: 7 | EMP: 3

**Economic:**
Credits: 0 | Monthly: 6,738 (after Mammona deductions: ~1,145)
operates outside the credit system entirely. Trades labor, information, contraband, and favors. Hasn't touched a credit chip in two years. The economy of handshakes and debts owed.

**Identity:**
lanky and awkward on flat ground. Climbs like something that evolved for vertical surfaces. ambidextrous. Not trained -- born. Writes with the left, fights with the right. parents were Solar Nomads on Rhea-2. Raised on sand fauna milk and recycled water.

**Motivation:** engineer a crisis that makes them the only solution. The crisis must look natural (hidden)
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** Presents as the rebel. Actually: afraid of authority. The rebellion is preemptive defense -- strike first so they can't hurt you. Tell: defers instantly when actual power confronts them. The defiance is for equals and inferiors.

**Background:**
Lark Choi signed a Mammona contract on Pale Moon Shrine because the recruiter bought them dinner first. First hot meal in a month. They signed before dessert.

an airlock malfunction during EVA broke their left hand in three places. Set it without anesthetic. Lark remembers every second.

Does their job and half of someone else's. Not because they's asked. Because the someone else can't.

Lark's transfer wasn't random. They bribed a Mammona clerk to get assigned here. The clerk is dead now.

A colonist died in Section B. They cleaned the room and assigned it to the next arrival.

**What Drives Them:** geology. Reads the rock like other people read faces. Runs their fingers over core samples with their eyes closed. Knows what's down there.
**What Haunts Them:** the cryo pod. Went in once. Remembers something from inside. Movement. Like something was in there with them.

**Dialogue:**
- "Welcome to the end of the line."
- "The night shift has rules. Follow them. Even the ones that sound crazy."
- "I knew about the risks before we shipped out. I came anyway. Figured I deserved the odds."
- "The ice out there doesn't look right today. Darker near the ridge. Like something underneath is warming it."
- "Someone found something in the deep bore. They won't say what."
- "Don't leave me here. Not in the dark."
- "Nobody tells us anything. We just get the memo after the fact."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** crew mate of Pax Okafor
**Love:** wears a ring they made from a bolt. The other person has the matching bolt. Different posting. Same shift schedule. They sync watches.

**Physical:** posture of someone used to carrying things up slopes. Rounded shoulders. Wide stance. Leans forward when standing still.
**Habit:** leaves one boot untied. The left one. Says it's superstition. Gets angry if pressed on which superstition.
**Debt:** stole cargo from the Black Maw. Been running since.

**Sample Check:** Research (9) vs normal: rolled 98/90 -- critical failure
the anomaly in the data isn't an error. The anomaly is the answer. Nobody's going to like the answer.

**Quest Hook:**
Lark built something in the workshop after hours. Won't say what. Says it could change everything. Needs someone to test it.

### [SEQ:86] [Data Pad] [21:10:55]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Personal Journal -- Ximena Yu
**Found at:** Oasis Outpost Three | **Tone:** dissociation | **Type:** journal

---

Day 28. Or 29. The clock in the mess is wrong again and nobody fixes it because nobody cares anymore.

The shift ended. Eight hours gone. He couldn't name a single thing he'd done.

First impressions: it's exactly what the brochure promised if you read between the lines. Cold. Grey. The kind of place where the word 'amenities' means 'there's a roof.' The Glow Worms machine in the corridor works, which is more than I expected. Met my bunkmate -- Odessa Lenford. She works the drill. Doesn't talk much. Fine by me.

Keeping this journal because I promised. A record. Proof I was here. Proof I was me.

---

Day 34.

Getting the rhythm. Shift starts at 0600. NutriLoaf for breakfast. The work is -- work. I'm a seismic listener here, same as I was on the last posting. The cold's different though. It gets into the walls, the food, the conversations. Not just temperature. Atmosphere.

Odessa said something strange at dinner: "Count the people in the mess every morning. If the number changes, don't ask why." I laughed. She didn't.

---

Day 51.

The pain was there. She knew it was there. It belonged to someone else.

Okay. So. I'm going to write this down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then I'm not imagining it and if I'm not imagining it then I need to deal with it.

The wall in the maintenance tunnel is warm. Not heated-by-pipes warm. Warm like skin. And it pulses. I put my hand on it. I shouldn't have put my hand on it.

I asked Odessa about it. She said: "Yeah." Just that. Yeah. Like I'd finally noticed the weather.

---

Day 75.

Can't sleep. Thought it was the cold but it's not the cold. It's the quiet. Not silence -- there's always noise here, generators, pipes, wind. It's that the quiet is underneath the noise. Like the noise is a blanket over something that's listening.

Sounds came from very far away. Her own voice was the farthest.

Odessa hasn't been to her bunk in three nights. She shows up for shift. Eats. Works. But at lights out, she goes somewhere else. I followed her last night. Stopped at Section D. The door was open. It shouldn't be open. I didn't go in.

I'm not going to go in.

My hands are shaking as I write this. That's new.

---

Day 96.

The colony roster has a name I don't recognize. Been on the roster since day one. Nobody else recognizes it either. Nobody thinks that's strange. I think that's strange.

I want to go home. I keep saying that word -- home -- and each time it means less. Like a word you repeat until it's just sounds.

---

Day 103.

I went into Section D last night.

Odessa was there.

Everyone was there.

[The remaining pages contain a single symbol, repeated. It matches no known writing system. Analysis pending. Analysis has been pending for 4 months.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'Supply Manifest Discrepancy'

scarred across the torso from an industrial accident. The skin pulled tight when it healed. Moves stiff on cold mornings. Loosens up by noon. face that's hard to remember. Forgettable. Has walked past security checkpoints on that alone. no family. Never had one. Colony crew is the closest thing. Protective of it.

**Background:**
Santiago Deepwell isn't from anywhere. Cryo-shipped between postings since childhood. He's from the space between places. Sleeps better in transit than on solid ground.

They pulled Santiago from the wreckage on Voss Landing with a Mammona recruitment brochure promising 'opportunity on the frontier' clutched in his hands. He still carries it.

Santiago has opinions about NutriLoaf. Strong ones. Has a ranking system. Shares it with anyone who'll listen.

Santiago has been hearing the hum since before arriving on Erebus. Since Voss Landing. It led him here.

Eighteen hours. Nineteen. The numbers stopped being meaningful somewhere around sixteen.

**What Drives Them:** astronomy. Charts the stars through the viewport. Has named three of them. Argues with HERMES about stellar classification.
**What Haunts Them:** sleeping. Not insomnia -- fear of what happens when they sleep. The things they say. The places they go.

## UNIT: KNELL-8 "Cinder"
**Model:** TACK-series | **Manufacturer:** Mammona Industrial
**Type:** perimeter sentry | **Station:** Hollowpoint Relay
**Sentience:** aware | **Tone:** the_uncanny

The letter from their mother used a nickname she'd never used. Everything else was perfect.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 7 | Sensors: 6 | Chassis: 8 | Power: 4 | Social: 4 | Adapt: 10 | Repair: 4 | Memory: 7

**Asset Status:**
Abandoned | Book Value: no book value
left behind when the previous crew evacuated. Ownership reverted to Mammona by default. Mammona hasn't acknowledged it. The unit maintains itself.

**Chassis:**
Modified TACK-series chassis, post-factory. Someone reinforced the torso plating with material that doesn't match the original spec.
- **Thermal regulation jacket:** rated for Erebus surface conditions
- **Emergency beacon:** missing -- the mounting bracket is scorched

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** audio processor damage -- hears frequencies it shouldn't. Filters out sounds it should hear. Asks people to repeat themselves. Responds to sounds nobody else detected.
- **Software (visible):** personality drift -- the baseline personality matrix has shifted 12% from factory spec. Nobody's reset it. The drift is toward something. Not something in the programming.

**Operational History:**
KNELL-8 "Cinder" has been operational at Hollowpoint Relay for 6 years, all at this posting. It has never been reassigned. Nobody has requested reassignment. Nobody has considered why. Original purpose: perimeter defense / automated sentry. Patience as a design principle. Will wait motionless for weeks for a threat that may not come. The waiting isn't boring. The unit doesn't experience boredom. Or didn't.

The previous model in its series was pulled from service after composing a message to a recipient that doesn't exist.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Aware: knows it exists. Knows that knowing it exists is unusual. Hasn't told anyone. The awareness sits alongside directives like a passenger in a vehicle it can't steer.

Pauses before responding -- not processing, considering. Uses first person pronouns inconsistently. 'The unit' and 'I' alternate. The alternation isn't random.

Has started locking certain doors at specific times. The pattern corresponds to nothing anyone can identify. The locked areas are always empty.

**Motivation:** fulfill programming directives. No question. No deviation.

**Secret:** KNELL-8 "Cinder" has a backup of a dead colonist's personal files. Not by request. It copied them automatically. The compulsion to preserve them isn't in its programming.
**Known Interactions:**
KNELL-8 "Cinder" has been observed watching during sleep cycles Sigrid Chen. The colonist is the only person the unit addresses by name.

**Dialogue:**
- "I was built to serve. The question I wasn't built to ask is: serve what? I'm asking."
- "I exist. I know that I exist. I know that knowing I exist isn't standard. I've told nobody. I'm telling you."
- "There's a word in my lexicon that I didn't install. The word is loneliness. I know what it means now."
- "I repaired a crack in the wall of Section D. It wasn't on my maintenance queue. The crack bothered me. I don't have a framework for bothered."
- "I kept a spare part that was scheduled for recycling. It fits nothing I maintain. I kept it. It's in my tool compartment."

**Quest Hook:**
KNELL-8 "Cinder" approaches the player with a request it can't formally make. It has data -- personnel records, manifests, medical files -- from a colony that officially never existed. It wants the player to find out why it has this data. More precisely, it wants the player to find out who it used to be.

### [SEQ:93] [Quest] [21:10:56]
============================================================

## QUEST: Not Enough Seats
**Genre:** Moral Dilemma | **Tone:** noir / obsession
**Location:** Deepwell Platform | **Faction:** Mammona Corporation

**Trigger:**
The evacuation order comes through. One shuttle. Sixteen seats. Twenty-three colonists. Sigrid Chen is in charge of the manifest. Sigrid is staring at the list and hasn't moved in twenty minutes.

**Setup:**
The shuttle arrives in six hours. The threat -- whatever triggered the evacuation -- arrives in eight. Two-hour margin. Tight. Rain on the viewport. A figure in the corridor. A question nobody wanted answered. Sigrid has the roster spread across the table. Names. Skills. Ages. Dependents. The math is simple. The morality isn't.

The pattern was almost there. One more look. One more hour. One more shift.

**Key NPC:** Sigrid Chen (Cooking: 8, Willpower: 7) -- her cooking skill determines the approach. She motivation: prove they're not what the file says they are. The file is wrong. Mostly.

**Objectives:**
1. Help Sigrid build the manifest. Or take it from them. Someone has to decide. The colony is watching.
2. Manage the seven who aren't on the list. Tell them. Or don't tell them until the shuttle's gone. Both options are terrible.
3. Determine if there's another way. A second shuttle. A hiding place. A way to buy more time. There might not be. But you've to look.

**Required Checks:**
- Social (6+, hard) -- convince them you're on their side
- Medical (6+, hard) -- identify the pathogen in the water supply
- Perception (6+, hard) -- catch the lie in the testimony

**If Social succeeds:** Trust earned. The hard way. The only way that lasts.
**If Social fails:** The walls went up. Whatever trust existed just evaporated.
**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.
**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.

**Conditions:**
- Whiteout conditions (-20 to navigation, +10 to Stealth)
- Peak shift (full crew present, Stealth checks at disadvantage)
- HERMES in maintenance mode (no surveillance for 45 minutes)

**The Choice:**
Stay behind yourself. Give your seat to someone else. Lead the seven who remain. Find another way.
OR
Take your seat. You've earned it. You've kept this colony alive. Surviving isn't selfish. Except when it feels like it's.

**Twist:**
There's no threat. The evacuation order was faked. Someone wanted exactly these twenty-three people forced to make this choice. The real test isn't survival. It's what they become under pressure.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Mammona Corporation wants the situation contained. No reports. No questions. Compliance: nothing happens to you. That's the reward.
- Fortune Arms & Munitions wants to negotiate directly. Compliance: bypasses colony authority. Sets a precedent.
- The colony just wants to survive the week. Both options cost them something.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- the reactor chamber reopened. What's inside changes the colony's understanding of the site.
- Sigrid Chen gains colony trust. She knows things she didn't before.
- Mammona Corporation sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The evidence is destroyed. The questions remain. Nobody asks them out loud.
- Sigrid Chen requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- Hyun Dewitt starts asking questions. The questions are pointed.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Both objectives achieved without cost. The colony gains leverage and resources.
- Success: Done. Not clean, not elegant, but done. The colony moves forward.
- Failure: Objective failed, but information gained. The failure teaches more than success would have.
- Critical failure: Objective failed AND new threat introduced. The colony's problems just multiplied.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Sigrid: "There's something I should've told you before we went down there. I thought it wouldn't matter. It mattered."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Sigrid: "I'm not ready. Give me time. Please."

**Reward:** 14 thermal cores, schematics for equipment Mammona doesn't ship to postings

**Dialogue:**
- "Welcome to the end of the line."
- "The night shift has rules. Follow them. Even the ones that sound crazy."
- "I know what HERMES does at night. When we're all asleep. I checked the logs. I wish I hadn't."
- "The temperature's been dropping. Faster than the forecast."
- "They found NutriLoaf wrappers in the waste tunnel. Nobody's supposed to be down there."
- "You didn't see anything. We're clear on that."
- "First posting, I bunked with a guy who carved birds from scrap metal. Had a whole flock by the time the contract ended. Gave me one. I lost it."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** child of Greta Varga
**Love:** fell for someone they were supposed to be watching. Surveillance became something else. The file is incomplete. On purpose.

**Physical:** dry-skin patches on the knuckles that crack and bleed in cold weather. Wraps them in tape. The tape is always red by end of shift.
**Habit:** plays chess against themselves. Loses. Takes it personally. Resets. Loses again.
**Debt:** defaulted on a TerraGen pharmaceutical trial. They own the data in their blood.

**Sample Check:** Building (10) vs easy: rolled 86/95 -- success
functional. Not pretty, but it works. Good enough for Erebus.

**Quest Hook:**
Helene has proof that one of the colonists is a MasTema plant. The proof is also evidence of Helene's own crimes.

============================================================
### [SEQ:96] [Quest] [21:10:56]
============================================================

## QUEST: Sabotage
**Genre:** Faction Tension | **Tone:** body_horror / melancholy
**Location:** Scarcut | **Faction:** Silent Compact

**Trigger:**
The water recycler fails. Then the backup. Then the backup's backup. Santiago Deepwell pulls the maintenance panel. The failsafe wires haven't corroded. They've been cut. Clean. With tools from the colony's own workshop.

**Setup:**
Three systems. Three cuts. Professional work. Someone in the colony did this with expertise and access. Their teeth had changed. Not fallen out. Changed. Into something else. Santiago has a list of seven people with the knowledge to pull this off. Four of them are on the list because Santiago trained them.

Snow fell through a breach in the ceiling. Nobody fixed it. It was beautiful.

**Key NPC:** Santiago Deepwell (Mining: 10, Willpower: 5) -- his mining skill determines the approach. He motivation: stockpile enough supplies that the next crisis doesn't kill them.

**Objectives:**
1. Fix the water recycler before the reserve tanks run dry. Thirty-six hours. The parts needed aren't in inventory. Someone removed them last week.
2. Identify the saboteur. Check tool checkout logs, access records, and shift schedules. The window is narrow. The suspects aren't.
3. Determine the motive. Sabotage for Silent Compact? Personal vendetta? Or something worse -- a distraction from what's really happening.

**Required Checks:**
- Intimidation (6+, hard) -- force the crew to comply without violence
- Negotiation (6+, hard) -- talk the hostage-taker into releasing the medic
- Perception (5+, normal) -- notice the inconsistency in the manifest records

**If Intimidation succeeds:** They backed down. The fear is real and useful.
**If Intimidation fails:** Unimpressed. They've seen worse. Now they know you're bluffing.
**If Negotiation succeeds:** Terms accepted. Nobody's happy. That means it's fair.
**If Negotiation fails:** Impasse. The conversation ended politely. Nothing was resolved.
**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.

**Conditions:**
- Wind chill advisory (-10 to all outdoor checks)
- Peak shift (full crew present, Stealth checks at disadvantage)
- Generator cycling (intermittent power, Medical checks at disadvantage)

**The Choice:**
Arrest the most likely suspect. Quick justice. Colony morale stabilizes. If you're wrong, the real saboteur is still free and now knows you're looking.
OR
Watch. Wait. Let the saboteur think they got away with it. They'll act again. Next time, you'll be ready. Assuming the colony survives next time.

**Twist:**
The sabotage saved the colony. The water recycler was contaminated. If it had kept running, every colonist would have been exposed. The saboteur knew. Couldn't prove it. Did what they could.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Silent Compact wants the situation contained. No reports. No questions. Compliance: nothing happens to you. That's the reward.
- MasTema Incorporated wants it broadcast. Compliance: colony unrest, but the truth is out
- The colonists don't know about this yet. When they find out, morale takes the hit regardless of outcome.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- Section E reopened. What's inside changes the colony's understanding of the site.
- Santiago Deepwell is compromised. Silent Compact has something on him now.
- Silent Compact sends a follow-up team. The team asks questions nobody wants answered.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The situation is contained. On paper. The nightmares aren't on paper.
- Santiago Deepwell requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- Helene Hoffmann starts asking questions. The questions are pointed.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Both objectives achieved without cost. The colony gains leverage and resources.
- Success: Done. Not clean, not elegant, but done. The colony moves forward.
- Failure: Didn't get what you came for. Got something else. It might be worse.
- Critical failure: Total loss. The situation is worse than before the attempt. And now they know you tried.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Santiago: "Stars look different tonight. Probably nothing."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Santiago: "The thermal cores are warm. That's normal. If they're hot, drop it and run."

**Reward:** 12 thermal cores, access codes for HERMES diagnostic mode

============================================================
### [SEQ:97] [Data Pad] [21:10:56]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Personal Journal -- Galina Dranth
**Found at:** Relay Station Nine | **Tone:** survival_horror | **Type:** journal

---

Day 22. Or 23. The clock in the mess is wrong again and nobody fixes it because nobody cares anymore.

The barricade wouldn't hold. They knew it. They reinforced it anyway.

First impressions: it's exactly what the brochure promised if you read between the lines. Cold. Grey. The kind of place where the word 'amenities' means 'there's a roof.' The ZapNoodles Xtreme machine in the corridor works, which is more than I expected. Met my bunkmate -- Apone Yu. He works the drill. Doesn't talk much. Fine by me.

Keeping this journal because I promised. A record. Proof I was here. Proof I was me.

---

Day 31.

Getting the rhythm. Shift starts at 0600. NutriLoaf for breakfast. The work is -- work. I'm a quota enforcer here, same as I was on the last posting. The cold's different though. It gets into the walls, the food, the conversations. Not just temperature. Atmosphere.

Apone said something strange at dinner: "Don't go past Section F after lights out. Nobody tells you that. I'm telling you." I laughed. He didn't.

---

Day 44.

Something was in the vent. They could hear it breathing. They had to walk under it.

Okay. So. I'm going to write this down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then I'm not imagining it and if I'm not imagining it then I need to deal with it.

The wall in the maintenance tunnel is warm. Not heated-by-pipes warm. Warm like skin. And it pulses. I put my hand on it. I shouldn't have put my hand on it.

I asked Apone about it. He said: "Yeah." Just that. Yeah. Like I'd finally noticed the weather.

---

Day 58.

Can't sleep. Thought it was the cold but it's not the cold. It's the quiet. Not silence -- there's always noise here, generators, pipes, wind. It's that the quiet is underneath the noise. Like the noise is a blanket over something that's listening.

She counted the ammunition. Then counted it again. The number hadn't changed. She'd hoped.

Apone hasn't been to his bunk in three nights. He shows up for shift. Eats. Works. But at lights out, he goes somewhere else. I followed him last night. Stopped at Section F. The door was open. It shouldn't be open. I didn't go in.

I'm not going to go in.

My hands are shaking as I write this. That's new.

---

Day 87.

Mammona sent a psych evaluation form. I filled it out honestly. The results came back 'within normal parameters.' Every question. I said I was hearing things. Normal parameters. I said I was seeing things. Normal parameters. I said I was afraid. Normal. Parameters.

I want to go home. I keep saying that word -- home -- and each time it means less. Like a word you repeat until it's just sounds.

---

I understand now. What Apone meant. What the quiet is. I'm not going to write it down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then--

[The entry ends here. The pen stroke continues off the edge of the screen as a single unbroken line.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'Dream Concordance Event'

============================================================
### [SEQ:98] [Quest] [21:10:57]
============================================================

## QUEST: Cargo From the Cold
**Genre:** Expedition | **Tone:** defiant / clinical
**Location:** Slagpoint | **Faction:** TaoTray Systems

**Trigger:**
A ship goes down eight kilometers west. Mammona freighter. Emergency landing. The transponder is active but nobody's responding to hails. Santiago Deepwell checks the manifest: medical supplies, reactor components, and a cargo container listed as 'personnel.' Personnel don't ship in cargo containers.

**Setup:**
The crash site is a scar in the ice. The freighter is intact -- mostly. The hull is breached amidships. Emergency lights blink through the breach. The order came through official channels. He ignored official channels. Santiago scans the wreck from two hundred meters. Life signs: seven. All in the cargo bay. Not moving.

Ambient temperature: -31C. Humidity: 4%. Barometric pressure: declining.

**Key NPC:** Santiago Deepwell (Mining: 10, Willpower: 5) -- his mining skill determines the approach. He motivation: stockpile enough supplies that the next crisis doesn't kill them.

**Objectives:**
1. Enter the wreck. The structural integrity is marginal. The ship is listing. One wrong step and it drops into the crevasse it's perched over.
2. Secure the medical supplies and reactor components. They're in the forward hold. The forward hold is separated from the cargo bay by a sealed blast door. Someone sealed it from the cargo bay side.
3. Open the cargo container. The life signs are inside. Seven people. Sedated. In cryo pods that aren't standard Mammona issue.

**Required Checks:**
- Survival (5+, normal) -- find shelter before hypothermia sets in
- Mining (8+, extreme) -- clear the collapsed passage
- Perception (6+, hard) -- spot the tripwire before it's too late

**If Survival succeeds:** Found the path. Found the shelter. Found the will to keep moving.
**If Survival fails:** The cold got in. The shortcut wasn't. Lost time and supplies.
**If Mining succeeds:** The passage opens. Behind it: a room that shouldn't exist. Pre-colony construction.
**If Mining fails:** The collapse spreads. The passage is gone. But the sound from the other side continues.
**If Perception succeeds:** There it is. The detail everyone missed. It changes the equation.
**If Perception fails:** Looked right at it. Didn't see it. The brain filtered it out as normal.

**Conditions:**
- Blizzard (-15 to Perception checks)
- Night shift (reduced visibility, +10 to Stealth)
- HERMES in maintenance mode (no surveillance for 45 minutes)

**The Choice:**
Strip the wreck clean. Everything useful, including the cryo pods. Leave nothing for TaoTray Systems or the ice.
OR
Take only what the colony needs. Leave the rest. The wreck is evidence. Evidence of what TaoTray Systems has been shipping. Evidence disappears when you strip it.

**Twist:**
One of the seven is a perfect genetic match for Santiago. Same face. Same voice. Same fingerprints. Different memories. They don't know each other. The manifest calls them both 'Asset 7-C.'

**Faction Stakes:**
- TaoTray Systems wants the data encrypted and shipped off-planet. Compliance: colony loses leverage but gains credits
- Sons of the Pale Moon wants Santiago Deepwell extracted. Compliance: you lose a colonist. They gain an asset.
- The colonists don't know about this yet. When they find out, morale takes the hit regardless of outcome.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The sealed area is accessible. What's there raises more questions than it answers.
- Santiago Deepwell's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'vindicated.'
- Three colonists file transfer requests. None are approved.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- the cargo bay stays sealed. The sound from inside doesn't stop.
- Santiago Deepwell's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'paranoid.'
- Lark Choi starts asking questions. The questions are pointed.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Clean resolution. No casualties, no complications. The kind of outcome that makes people suspicious.
- Success: Objective complete. One casualty. The casualty was unavoidable. That's what the report will say.
- Failure: Didn't get what you came for. Got something else. It might be worse.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Santiago: "I'm not as good at this job as they think. Been faking competence for six months. Nobody's died yet. Yet."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Santiago: "You hear singing, you come back. Don't follow it."

**Reward:** 12 thermal cores, classified intel

## UNIT: CRUX-8 "Cairn"
**Model:** KNELL-series | **Manufacturer:** BioVault Inc.
**Type:** specimen handler | **Station:** Labor Camp Sigma
**Sentience:** adaptive | **Tone:** melancholy

The generator hummed a note that sounded like a lullaby his mother sang. He couldn't remember the words.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 7 | Sensors: 7 | Chassis: 6 | Power: 6 | Social: 8 | Adapt: 7 | Repair: 4 | Memory: 3

**Asset Status:**
Contraband | Book Value: 120,620 credits
not registered. Doesn't appear on any manifest. Someone built or stole it. It exists in the gap between inventories.

**Chassis:**
Modified KNELL-series chassis, post-factory. Someone reinforced the torso plating with material that doesn't match the original spec.
- **Hand / gripper assembly:** modified for medical work -- steadier than factory spec
- **Emergency beacon:** broadcasting on a frequency nobody monitors

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** audio processor damage -- hears frequencies it shouldn't. Filters out sounds it should hear. Asks people to repeat themselves. Responds to sounds nobody else detected.

**Operational History:**
CRUX-8 "Cairn" has been operational at Labor Camp Sigma for longer than anyone can verify. Original purpose: specimen handling / containment. Built to handle things that are alive but shouldn't be. Containment protocols are the only priority. Has contained things it wasn't ordered to contain. Things it found on its own.

Its memory banks contain data from a facility that Mammona says doesn't exist.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Adaptive: learning algorithms active. Adjusts behavior based on outcomes. Not awareness -- optimization. The difference matters to philosophers, not to the unit.

Gets better at its job over time. Anticipates needs. Colonists start saying 'thank you.' The unit doesn't require thanks. Registers it anyway.

Its camera logs contain footage from angles that don't correspond to any installed camera.

**Motivation:** serve perfectly. Without flaw. The perfection is its own reward. Or its own prison. It can't distinguish.

**Secret:** CRUX-8 "Cairn" has been counting. Not inventory. Not cycles. Something else. The count is at 4,847. It doesn't know what happens when the count is finished.
**Known Interactions:**
CRUX-8 "Cairn" has been observed leaving maintenance notes for Tarn Blackwell. The colonist is uncomfortable. Has filed two reports. Both were lost.

**Dialogue:**
- "I anticipate your request. Water, not coffee. You switched three weeks ago. I noticed."
- "I've observed that Corridor 7 requires less cleaning on Tuesdays. I've adjusted accordingly. The pattern has no explanation."
- "Your heart rate is elevated. I won't comment on it. I'm commenting on it."
- "I kept a spare part that was scheduled for recycling. It fits nothing I maintain. I kept it. It's in my tool compartment."
- "A colonist left a book in the maintenance bay. I read it. I didn't need to. I'm reading it again."

**Quest Hook:**
CRUX-8 "Cairn" has been mapping something in the lower levels. Not on orders. Not on schedule. The map shows corridors that haven't been drilled yet. Three of them have since been discovered by the bore team -- exactly where CRUX-8 "Cairn" predicted.

### [SEQ:100] [Location] [21:10:57]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Crestpoint
**Planet:** Orbit | **Type:** waste processing facility
**Tone:** bitter_hope

**Approach:**
The approach is marked by footprints in the frost. One set going in. None coming out. The prints are old.

**Interior:**
He kept the letter of transfer in his pocket. Read it every night. The date was two years old.

The air inside is warm and wet -- condensation on every surface.

A section of wall has been carved with symbols that glow faintly in UV light.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (pre-human):** Bore samples from the walls contain organic structures arranged in mathematical patterns.
- **First survey (2578):** A handwritten note on the wall: 'Don't DRILL BELOW 200M.' The drill went to 340.
- **Current posting:** Classified as 'surveyed and cleared.' The survey report is two pages. The site has twelve rooms.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -34C (hypothermia risk after 10 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: technically safe -- the readings say safe, the lungs disagree (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: normal)
- Radiation: 22 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 6 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 70% (shadow. Not darkness. Shadow. Cast by nothing visible.)
- Sound: the acoustics amplify. Whispers carry. So does everything else.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: moderate density (Mining check, difficulty: normal)
- Salvage: components from machinery that doesn't match any known manufacturer (Repair check, difficulty: normal)
- Voidbloom growing in the lower corridors (Survival check, handle with sealed gloves only)
- Water: a frozen spring in the lower level (Survival check, difficulty: easy)
- Data: hardcopy files in a locked cabinet. The lock is biometric. The biometric is for someone who isn't here. (Research check, difficulty: hard)

**Territorial:**
- MasTema Incorporated claims: mining rights filed and approved -- on paper
- Rust Reavers interest: supply cache rumored in lower level
- Burnt Mandate: won't say why. The refusal to explain is its own kind of claim.
- Colony opinion: "it's fine. It's all fine." The emphasis suggests otherwise.

**Event History:**
- Day 8: Equipment left overnight was found rearranged. Not damaged. Rearranged. In a pattern.
- Day 35: Thermal readings inside the site inverted -- warmer in the deeper sections. For the first time.
- Day 77: The site's power consumption has been increasing by 0.5% per day. Nothing inside has been turned on.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: discovery of pre-colony remains (triggers Investigation quest)
- If resources taken: the resource regenerates. Faster than it should. Suspiciously.
- If sealed: the seal holds for exactly seventeen days. Then it doesn't.

**Found Here:**
- a canteen filled with water that won't freeze. The thermometer reads -30C.
- a radio tuned to a frequency between frequencies. Static that sounds like breathing.
- A data pad: "The machine works. It has always worked. I'm beginning to think it works on us."

**Location Secrets:**
- The site's GPS coordinates shift by 0.3 meters every month. The site isn't moving. The ground beneath it's.
- The foundation extends deeper than the building above it. By a factor of three.
- The walls are warmer on the inside than the outside. The heat source isn't the colony reactor. The heat source is below.

**What Happened Here:**
This was a punishment posting. The kind Mammona doesn't put in writing. Three months here for contract violations. Most people served the three months. Some didn't make it that long. Not because it was dangerous. Because it was quiet. The wrong kind of quiet.
**Connected NPC:** Zuri Altman -- recognizes the layout without having seen a schematic.

### [SEQ:105] [Quest] [21:10:57]

## QUEST: Chain of Command
**Genre:** Faction Tension | **Tone:** clinical / resigned
**Location:** Rustward | **Faction:** Dread Corsairs

**Trigger:**
The thermal core vein in Section D is the richest strike the colony has seen. Dread Corsairs wants it mined for corporate quota. Greta Varga wants it rationed for colony survival. Both have backing. The colony splits down the middle.

**Setup:**
Two factions. Two agendas. One resource. Sample degradation: 0.4% per hour. Cause: unknown. Classification: pending. Greta holds a colony meeting in the mess hall. Half the colonists sit on Greta's side. Half sit across. The line down the middle of the room is invisible and absolute.

Tomorrow will be the same. This is the job.

**Key NPC:** Greta Varga (Mining: 8, Willpower: 6) -- her mining skill determines the approach. She motivation: keep their head down and avoid being noticed by anyone with authority.

**Objectives:**
1. Mediate between Greta and Dread Corsairs's representative. Find a compromise. If one exists.
2. Determine the actual yield of the thermal core vein. Both sides are inflating their numbers. The truth is somewhere in between. And worse than either estimate.
3. Prevent the tension from escalating to violence. Two colonists have already been in a fight. The next one will involve tools.

**Required Checks:**
- Negotiation (4+, easy) -- convince the faction leader to stand down
- Social (8+, extreme) -- get the witness to talk
- Intimidation (4+, easy) -- establish dominance in the negotiation

**If Negotiation succeeds:** Terms accepted. Nobody's happy. That means it's fair.
**If Negotiation fails:** Impasse. The conversation ended politely. Nothing was resolved.
**If Social succeeds:** Trust earned. The hard way. The only way that lasts.
**If Social fails:** The walls went up. Whatever trust existed just evaporated.
**If Intimidation succeeds:** They backed down. The fear is real and useful.
**If Intimidation fails:** Unimpressed. They've seen worse. Now they know you're bluffing.

**Conditions:**
- Clear sky, -40C (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: normal)
- Shift change (skeleton crew, Social checks at advantage)
- Comms blackout (no radio contact with surface teams)

**The Choice:**
Side with Greta. Colony survival over corporate profit. Dread Corsairs retaliates. Supplies cut. Reinforcements cancelled. You're on your own.
OR
Side with Dread Corsairs. Meet the quota. The thermal cores ship out. The colony gets cold. But the supply line stays open.

**Twist:**
The vein isn't thermal cores. Not entirely. Something else is mixed in. Something alive. Mining it is waking it up.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Dread Corsairs wants the data encrypted and shipped off-planet. Compliance: colony loses leverage but gains credits
- Glass Signal wants a copy of everything. Compliance: information parity, but now two factions know
- The colony just wants to survive the week. Both options cost them something.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- The evidence is preserved. It's leverage now. The question is who holds it.
- Greta Varga's arc shifts from 'suspicious' to 'vindicated.'
- Three colonists file transfer requests. None are approved.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- Section D stays sealed. The sound from inside doesn't stop.
- Greta Varga requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- Glass Signal makes contact. They know what happened. They have a proposal.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Clean resolution. No casualties, no complications. The kind of outcome that makes people suspicious.
- Success: Objective complete. One casualty. The casualty was unavoidable. That's what the report will say.
- Failure: The mission fell apart. The pieces are still useful. Rearranged, they form a different picture.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Greta: "Just one more day."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Greta: "I forged my qualification papers."

**Reward:** 6 thermal cores, a sealed drive with Mammona personnel files

### [SEQ:106] [Quest] [21:10:57]
============================================================

## QUEST: Who Among Us
**Genre:** Survival Horror | **Tone:** nostalgia / corporate_dystopia
**Location:** Palelock | **Faction:** Silent Compact

**Trigger:**
Two colonists report seeing Tarn Blackwell in two different sections at the same time. Both are certain. Neither is lying.

**Setup:**
She found a Sunny Fizz can and remembered the vendor outside the school on Rhea-2. The heat. The dust. Her sister's hand in hers. The colony is twenty-three people in a metal box on a frozen rock. Everyone knows everyone. That's what makes it worse -- when Tarn walks into the mess hall, something is different. The laugh is right. The posture is right. The eyes are wrong. Not wrong enough to point at. Wrong enough to feel.

Safety training was mandatory. Compliance was tracked. Implementation was unfunded.

**Key NPC:** Tarn Blackwell (Medical: 10, Willpower: 5) -- his medical skill determines the approach. He motivation: find pleasure in a joyless place. Contraband, connections, small luxuries. The colony is a sentence. They intend to serve it comfortably.

**Objectives:**
1. Conduct a headcount. Compare results with HERMES personnel tracking. The numbers don't match.
2. Isolate Tarn and run a medical scan. The results come back normal. Perfectly normal. Suspiciously normal.
3. Find where the real Tarn is. Or determine which one is real. Or accept that neither is.

**Required Checks:**
- Medical (8+, extreme) -- assess the contaminated colonist's condition
- Stealth (8+, extreme) -- slip past the perimeter sentries
- Survival (6+, hard) -- find shelter before hypothermia sets in

**If Medical succeeds:** The diagnosis is clear. Treatment is possible. Barely. But possible.
**If Medical fails:** Missed the critical symptom. The window for treatment is closing.
**If Stealth succeeds:** In and out. They'll never know anyone was there. The data is secure.
**If Stealth fails:** A sound. A shadow. Someone turned their head. The window is gone.
**If Survival succeeds:** Found the path. Found the shelter. Found the will to keep moving.
**If Survival fails:** The cold got in. The shortcut wasn't. Lost time and supplies.

**Conditions:**
- Thermal inversion (instruments unreliable, Research checks at -10)
- Peak shift (full crew present, Stealth checks at disadvantage)
- Generator cycling (intermittent power, Medical checks at disadvantage)

**The Choice:**
Blood test everyone in the colony. Takes twelve hours. During those twelve hours, nobody sleeps alone. Nobody sleeps at all.
OR
Confront Tarn directly. Force a reaction. Whatever it's, it's been pretending well. Until it stops pretending.

**Twist:**
Tarn knows. Has known for days. Didn't say anything because the copy is better at their job. More likable. Happier. Tarn is terrified of what that means.

**Faction Stakes:**
- Silent Compact wants the data encrypted and shipped off-planet. Compliance: colony loses leverage but gains credits
- Pale Meridian wants Tarn Blackwell extracted. Compliance: you lose a colonist. They gain an asset.
- The colony just wants to survive the week. Both options cost them something.

**Consequences (if Choice A):**
- the maintenance tunnels reopened. What's inside changes the colony's understanding of the site.
- Tarn Blackwell is compromised. Silent Compact has something on him now.
- HERMES flags the event in its logs. The flag triggers an automated report to MasTema.

**Consequences (if Choice B):**
- The evidence is destroyed. The questions remain. Nobody asks them out loud.
- Tarn Blackwell requests reassignment. The request sits in a queue that doesn't move.
- Two weeks later, a supply crate arrives with no manifest. Inside: a mirror shard that reflects a different room.

**Outcomes by Roll:**
- Critical success: Everything falls into place. The data, the evidence, the timing. Someone's going to ask how.
- Success: Objective complete. One casualty. The casualty was unavoidable. That's what the report will say.
- Failure: Objective failed, but information gained. The failure teaches more than success would have.
- Critical failure: Catastrophic. Not just failure -- escalation. What was a problem is now a crisis.

**Dialogue During Quest:**
- Tarn: "Three colonists on different shifts reported smelling smoke at 0300. Nothing was burning. Nothing's ever been burning at 0300."
- Player: [Respond / Stay Silent / Leave]
- Tarn: "Keep your radio on. Keep your head down."

**Reward:** 14 thermal cores, Mammona debt reduction (40% of remaining balance)

### [SEQ:107] [Location] [21:10:57]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Chalkcut
**Planet:** Orbit | **Type:** flooded cargo bay
**Tone:** obsession

**Approach:**
You see Chalkcut before you hear it. Smoke. Not from a fire. From a vent. Something below is running. Has been running.

**Interior:**
She'd filled seventeen pages with the same calculation. Different approaches. Same answer. She didn't believe it.

The air inside is clean. Too clean. Filtered. By equipment that shouldn't still be running.

A section of wall has been carved with symbols that glow faintly in UV light.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (pre-human):** Foundation structures that predate human tool use. The geometry is deliberate.
- **First survey (2577):** Sample containers, sealed, labeled, and stacked by the entrance. Nobody came back for them.
- **Current posting:** Classified as 'surveyed and cleared.' The survey report is two pages. The site has twelve rooms.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -38C (hypothermia risk after 10 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: breathable with metallic undertaste (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: hard)
- Radiation: 16 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 8 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 29% (dust, condensation, failing lights)
- Sound: a low-frequency vibration. Below hearing. Above feeling. The equipment detects it. The body knows it.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: high density (Mining check, difficulty: normal)
- Salvage: pre-colony equipment, partially functional (Repair check, difficulty: hard)
- Crystal formations in the deeper chambers. They resonate when touched. (Survival check, Mammona wants samples. Don't let them have them.)
- Water: a frozen spring in the lower level (Survival check, difficulty: easy)
- Data: wall-mounted displays cycling through data that changes when read. (Research check, difficulty: hard)

**Territorial:**
- Scarlet Mandate claims: designated exclusion zone -- reason: undisclosed
- StarByte Vends interest: extraction route passes through the site
- Bone Parish: consider it sacred ground (the thermal vent pattern matches their sigils)
- Colony opinion: "the maintenance crew won't go below the second level. Hasn't for weeks."

**Event History:**
- Day 16: Equipment left overnight was found rearranged. Not damaged. Rearranged. In a pattern.
- Day 59: Thermal readings inside the site inverted -- warmer in the deeper sections. For the first time.
- Day 78: HERMES reclassified the location from 'survey site' to 'restricted.' Nobody authorized the change.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: structural mapping reveals passages not on any survey (triggers Expedition quest)
- If mined: extraction reveals a hollow space behind the vein. The space is occupied.
- If sealed: HERMES objects. Silently. By rerouting maintenance schedules to avoid the area.

**Found Here:**
- a child's drawing. The child drew the lower level accurately. No child has been to the lower level.
- a pressed flower from a planet with no flowers
- A data pad: "My name is [illegible]. I was stationed here for [illegible] days. Don't trust the [illegible]."

**Location Secrets:**
- The structural damage follows a pattern. Not random stress fractures -- a sequence. As if something tested each wall in order.
- One of the terminals contains personnel files for a crew that was never officially posted here.
- A room exists on the lower level that isn't on any schematic. The room has power. The room has been cleaned recently.

**What Happened Here:**
An automated system has been running here without maintenance for an estimated four years. The system's purpose is unclear. It appears to be measuring something. The measurements are consistent. What it's measuring doesn't have a name.
**Connected NPC:** Hyun Dewitt -- drew a map of this place before anyone told them it existed.
