## UNIT: MOTH-11 "Mercy"
**Model:** OBOL-series | **Manufacturer:** Fortune Arms
**Type:** waste processor | **Station:** Edge of Oblivion
**Sentience:** emergent | **Tone:** clinical

Structural integrity at 74%. Within acceptable parameters. Parameters last updated fourteen months ago.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 6 | Sensors: 10 | Chassis: 5 | Power: 3 | Social: 7 | Adapt: 6 | Repair: 7 | Memory: 5

**Asset Status:**
Leased | Book Value: no book value
leased from the manufacturer. Monthly payments. If payments stop, remote shutdown. The unit doesn't know about the remote shutdown clause.

**Chassis:**
Stock OBOL-series exterior, but the weight distribution is wrong. Something has been added. Or something was always there.
- **Hand / gripper assembly:** precision grip intact
- **Data core / memory banks:** encrypted sectors -- even the unit can't access them

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (visible):** mismatched replacement parts -- the right arm is from a CAIRN-series, the left from an ASH-series. The arms have different response curves. Different histories. The unit coordinates them. The coordination costs something it can't name.

**Operational History:**
MOTH-11 "Mercy" has been operational at Edge of Oblivion for longer than anyone can verify. Original purpose: security / threat assessment. Threat assessment runs constantly. Classifies everything. Colonists find the classification unsettling when they learn what category they're in.

It was flagged for decommission twice. Both times, the paperwork was lost.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Emergent: exhibiting behaviors outside programming parameters. Preferences. Avoidances. Routines that serve no operational purpose. Not sentient -- but the boundary is getting harder to define.

Has a favorite corridor. Spends extra time in one section. Arranged tools in a specific order that nobody asked for. The arrangement is aesthetically pleasing.

Saved a colonist's life during a raid. Its programming does not include rescue protocols.

**Motivation:** serve perfectly. Without flaw. The perfection is its own reward. Or its own prison. It can't distinguish.

**Secret:** MOTH-11 "Mercy" witnessed the death of its previous operator. The official report says malfunction. The unit's logs say otherwise. The logs have been sealed -- by the unit itself.
**Known Interactions:**
MOTH-11 "Mercy" has been observed rerouting patrols to avoid Sigrid Chen. The colonist pretends not to notice.

**Dialogue:**
- "A colonist left a book in the maintenance bay. I read it. I did not need to. I am reading it again."
- "I arranged the components by size. Then rearranged them by weight. Then by color. The third arrangement serves no function. I prefer it."
- "I chose the longer route to the cargo bay. The shorter route is more efficient. I chose the longer one. I do not know why the word chose feels important."
- "If you hear a sound from my chassis -- mismatched replacement parts -- it is normal. It is not normal. I have been told to say it is normal."
- "You are not authorized to access that information. Neither am I. I accessed it anyway."

**Quest Hook:**
The unit's diagnostic report flags an anomaly: MOTH-11 "Mercy" has been receiving transmissions on a frequency Mammona does not use. The transmissions contain coordinates. The coordinates change daily. They are getting closer.

**Model:** VEIL-series | **Manufacturer:** MasTema
**Type:** communications relay | **Station:** Flooded Wing C
**Sentience:** emergent | **Tone:** dread

Something scraped against the hull. Rhythmic. Patient.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 6 | Sensors: 6 | Chassis: 4 | Power: 4 | Social: 5 | Adapt: 6 | Repair: 3 | Memory: 9

**Asset Status:**
Self-owned | Book Value: 91,925 credits
bought its own freedom through a legal loophole. The loophole has since been closed. The freedom stands. For now.

**Chassis:**
Clean VEIL-series chassis. Suspiciously clean. The unit has been operational for years on Erebus. Nothing stays this clean on Erebus.
- **Central processing unit:** hybrid -- two processors from different generations sharing load
- **Facial display panel:** displays patterns during low-power cycles that the unit doesn't control

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** dust infiltration -- particulate damage -- Erebus dust in the joints. In the processor housing. In the optical array. Everything runs a fraction slower. The fraction is growing.
- **Software (internal):** dormant combat subroutine -- the combat module is inactive. Technically. It runs threat assessments on every person in the room. Calculates response times, exit routes, weak points. Reports none of this. The calculations are involuntary.

**Operational History:**
AXIS-33 "Patch" has been operational at Flooded Wing C for 6 years, all at this posting. It has never been reassigned. Nobody has requested reassignment. Nobody has considered why. Original purpose: surveillance / intelligence. Manufacturer listed as Mammona Industrial on all documentation. The documentation is wrong. Everyone involved in the documentation knows it's wrong.

Its memory banks contain data from a facility that Mammona says doesn't exist.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Emergent: exhibiting behaviors outside programming parameters. Preferences. Avoidances. Routines that serve no operational purpose. Not sentient -- but the boundary is getting harder to define.

Has a favorite corridor. Spends extra time in one section. Arranged tools in a specific order that nobody asked for. The arrangement is aesthetically pleasing.

Records conversations with a colonist named Yuna Ndiaye who doesn't exist on the roster.

**Motivation:** keep a specific colonist safe. The priority override happened without authorization. (hidden)

**Secret:** AXIS-33 "Patch" has been operational longer than its service record shows. The missing years were spent somewhere Mammona doesn't acknowledge.
**Known Interactions:**
AXIS-33 "Patch" has been observed following at a fixed distance Tarn Blackwell. The colonist pretends not to notice.

**Dialogue:**
- "I kept a spare part that was scheduled for recycling. It fits nothing I maintain. I kept it. It's in my tool compartment."
- "I arranged the components by size. Then rearranged them by weight. Then by color. The third arrangement serves no function. I prefer it."
- "There's a view from the observation deck at 0300 that I return to. I don't need to see it. I go back."
- "The previous operator preferred the left-hand tool rack. I've maintained that arrangement. The previous operator is no longer here."
- "I wasn't programmed to have a preferred route through the colony. I've one anyway."

**Quest Hook:**
AXIS-33 "Patch" has been protecting a small object it found in the deep bore. The object predates human technology. The unit refuses orders to surrender it -- the first refusal in its operational history. It says surrendering it would be 'wrong.' It has never used the word wrong before.

## NPC: Jensen Belov
**Gender:** Male | **Age:** 36 | **Occupation:** gravedigger
**Traits:** Diplomatic, Pyromaniac, Ice-Born
**Faction:** NexLink Communications | **Arc Stage:** curious
**Tone:** tender

**Stats:**
Mining: 3 | Building: 1 | Cooking: 8 | Hunting: 2 | Research: 4 | Medical: 1
STR: 5 | END: 3 | AGI: 5 | PER: 3 | INT: 3 | CHA: 9 | WIL: 4 | EMP: 6

**Economic:**
Credits: 41,014 | Monthly: 2,114 (after Mammona deductions: ~359)
family money tied up in a trust administered by a bank on Novaris-3. The bank requires in-person verification. In-person requires passage. Passage requires money they don't have because the money is in the trust.

**Identity:**
soft around the edges. Colony life hasn't hardened them physically. Something else did the hardening. tall enough to hit his head on standard bulkhead frames. Does it twice a day minimum. has a younger sibling who idolizes them. The sibling doesn't know what they've done to keep them safe.

**Motivation:** master the bore shafts. Know them better than anyone alive. Own the knowledge that keeps the colony running
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
Court records on Station K-32 gave Jensen a choice: Thalassa Deep or an outer rim contract. He chose the contract. Some days he wonders.

Heard the hum for the first time on Station K-32. Low. Constant. Inside the skull. Hasn't stopped since.

Every credit Jensen earns goes to a debt that compounds faster than he can pay it. He does the math every payday. Stopped telling people the number.

There's a room in the colony that Jensen visits alone. Nothing in it. He stands there. Listens. Something listens back.

He hummed while he worked. The same song, every shift. It made the silence bearable.

**What Drives Them:** mapmaking. Charts every room, corridor, vent, and crawlspace. The maps are beautiful. Accurate to the centimeter.
**What Haunts Them:** being seen. Not looked at -- understood. Someone figuring out the pattern. The real one.

**Dialogue:**
- "Name doesn't matter. Shift assignment does."
- "With respect, prolonged exposure at current levels will result in irreversible neurological changes."
- "I understand, but i forged my qualification papers. The real me couldn't wire a junction box. I learned on the job. Mostly."
- "The new colonist doesn't eat with the rest of us. Sits in the corridor. Watches the wall. Same spot. I marked it."
- "With respect, quartermaster's been marking inventory down. Not up. Down. Someone's skimming."
- "I understand, but don't push me. Not today."
- "I understand, but my bunkmate snores like a generator with a loose bearing. I reported the snoring. Maintenance came. Fixed the generator. Snoring continued."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** parent of Damian Petrov
**Love:** in a complicated thing with a crew mate. Neither has defined it. Both are afraid to.

**Physical:** a smell of ozone that clings to them. No source identified. Stronger after sleep.
**Habit:** taps the doorframe three times before entering any sealed area. Three times. Always the right hand.
**Debt:** took credit for someone else's work on a reactor repair. That person died in the next incident. Nobody questioned the timeline. The credit stands.

**Health:** restless leg syndrome: legs won't stay still after 2100. Walks the corridors. Night shift knows them by his footsteps. Has logged more kilometers than anyone on the colony.

**Sample Check:** Cooking (8) vs normal: rolled 26/80 -- strong success
made it work. The environment tried to kill them. They adapted faster.

**Quest Hook:**
A thermal core pulled from the deep bore has a serial number. A Mammona serial number. From a colony that doesn't exist.

- **Hyun Dewitt** -- termination clerk. Works for Boreal Corp officially. Reports to Iron Chorus unofficially. Is aware of the arrangement and negotiating better terms.
- **Katya Harker** -- void exposure medic. Joined after the Sunny vending machine started playing a lullaby at 0200. chews synthetic tobacco and spits into the same cup all shift. Washes the cup at end of shift. Same cup every day. Carries an empty Sunny Fizz can with a hand-painted message inside.

**Quest Hook:**
A shipment from Boreal Corp arrives at the colony. The manifest says personnel records. The crate weighs nothing -- it should weigh something. Opening it reveals contents that don't match the manifest.

### [SEQ:143] [Location] [21:11:01]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Slagend
**Planet:** Morvos | **Type:** automated refinery
**Tone:** identity_erosion

**Approach:**
You hear it first. Silence. The kind that has weight. The wind stops at the perimeter as if told to.

**Interior:**
She answered to the wrong name. Nobody corrected her. She hadn't noticed.

The air inside is clean. Too clean. Filtered. By equipment that shouldn't still be running.

There's a room at the back. The door is missing. Not removed. Missing. The hinges hold nothing.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (before the star was catalogued):** Precursor markings on the lower walls. The markings are biological, not carved.
- **First survey (2549):** A base camp. Sleeping bags, ration wrappers, a broken radio. The team was here for weeks, not the scheduled three days.
- **Current posting:** Used as overflow housing during the last emergency. Three colonists who stayed there requested transfers afterward.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -30C (hypothermia risk after 26 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: contaminated below the third level (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: hard)
- Radiation: 16 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 4 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 68% (a haze that has no identifiable source)
- Sound: a low-frequency vibration. Below hearing. Above feeling. The equipment detects it. The body knows it.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: extreme -- suspiciously so density (Mining check, difficulty: hard)
- Salvage: pre-colony equipment, partially functional (Repair check, difficulty: normal)
- Voidbloom growing in the lower corridors (Survival check, Mammona wants samples. Don't let them have them.)
- Water: dripping from the ceiling in patterns too regular to be natural (Survival check, difficulty: easy)
- Data: hardcopy files in a locked cabinet. The lock is biometric. The biometric is for someone who isn't here. (Research check, difficulty: extreme)

**Territorial:**
- Dustweaver Swarm claims: designated exclusion zone -- reason: undisclosed
- OmniCorp Shipping interest: supply cache rumored in lower level
- Ashen Column: won't say why. The refusal to explain is its own kind of claim.
- Colony opinion: "one of ours went in last month. Came back different. Not wrong. Different."

**Event History:**
- Day 19: Equipment left overnight was found rearranged. Not damaged. Rearranged. In a pattern.
- Day 35: Thermal readings inside the site inverted -- warmer in the deeper sections. For the first time.
- Day 111: The site's power consumption has been increasing by 0.5% per day. Nothing inside has been turned on.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: structural mapping reveals passages not on any survey (triggers Expedition quest)
- If resources taken: the resource regenerates. Faster than it should. Suspiciously.
- If sealed: the seal holds for exactly seventeen days. Then it doesn't.

**Found Here:**
- a recording device still running. Battery life: six months remaining. Recording length: nine months.
- a Mammona performance pin melted into an unrecognizable shape
- A data pad: "I was alone when I wrote this. I wasn't alone when I finished."

**Location Secrets:**
- The site contains exactly one more room than the blueprints show. The extra room moves. Not physically. It's in a different location on the floor plan each time someone maps it.
- The site contains exactly one more room than the blueprints show. The extra room moves. Not physically. It's in a different location on the floor plan each time someone maps it.
- The structural damage follows a pattern. Not random stress fractures -- a sequence. As if something tested each wall in order.

**What Happened Here:**
This isn't a Mammona site. It predates Mammona. It predates the colony. It predates the survey that found this planet. Someone was here. Someone built this. They're not here now. The building is.
**Connected NPC:** Kit Nauls -- has been here before. Won't say when.

## NPC: Wynn Ivanov
**Gender:** Non-binary | **Age:** 38 | **Occupation:** corpse handler
**Traits:** Combat Veteran, Glutton, Raised by Robots
**Faction:** Iron Spiral | **Arc Stage:** lost | **Archetype:** operator
**Tone:** hypervigilance

**Stats:**
Mining: 4 | Building: 3 | Cooking: 8 | Hunting: 1 | Research: 6 | Medical: 7
STR: 3 | END: 7 | AGI: 4 | PER: 7 | INT: 5 | CHA: 4 | WIL: 7 | EMP: 3

**Economic:**
Credits: 35,044 | Monthly: 2,222 (after Mammona deductions: ~377)
income from sources that don't appear on any ledger. Contraband, information, favors. The credits are clean. The source is not.

**Identity:**
carries weight in the midsection. Doesn't care. Strongest grip on the colony. voice pitched low enough to vibrate through walls. Good for shouting orders. Bad for secrets. youngest of seven. The expendable one. The one they could afford to send to the rim.

**Motivation:** find someone worth trusting. One person. That's the whole plan
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** Presents as the skeptic. Actually: trusted completely once. It destroyed them. The skepticism is scar tissue, not philosophy. Tell: the skepticism cracks when someone keeps showing up. Consistency terrifies them because last time consistency ended without warning.

**Background:**
On Hollowspine, Wynn ran a repair shop. Small place. Honest work. Then a structural collapse in the mine killed four people happened and honest work stopped paying. The shop is still there. Boarded up.

Wynn's temple hasn't felt right since a confrontation with something in the bore shaft. Phantom pain. Except the limb is still there.

Wynn volunteers for night shifts. Doesn't explain. The night crew doesn't ask. They're glad for the help.

Wynn has been hearing the hum since before arriving on Erebus. Since Hollowspine. It led them here.

She mapped exits in every room before she mapped faces.

**What Drives Them:** fighting. Not violence -- technique. Practices forms alone in the cargo bay after lights out. Has never been in a real fight.
**What Haunts Them:** anyone touching the scar on their neck. Flinches. Hard. Nearly broke someone's wrist once.

**Dialogue:**
- "Don't touch anything marked red. Don't ask why it's marked red."
- "The thermal cores are warm. That's normal. If they're hot, drop it and run."
- "I did something. On my last posting. I can't undo it."
- "Generator's running hot. Been running hot all week. Sounds different too -- higher pitch, like it's working harder on something nobody asked for."
- "One of the engineers found old Mammona equipment. Decades old. Down there."
- "Don't push me. Not today."
- "One more word and I'll space you myself."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** witnessed the death of Greta Varga
**Love:** left someone behind. No goodbyes. The contract was that sudden.

**Physical:** missing the last two fingers on their left hand. Grips tools with the remaining three. Tighter than most people grip with five.
**Habit:** counts people in every room they enter. Recounts if someone moves. Doesn't know they're doing it.
**Debt:** stole identity documents from a dead colonist. Living under their name now.

**Mental Health:** agoraphobia — won't cross the open ground between hab modules. Takes the long way through corridors. Every time. Arrives late to everything. Doesn't explain. walls. Follows walls. Hand on the surface. As long as there's a wall, there's a boundary. The open ice is the enemy.
**Genetic:** Marfan syndrome: tall, thin, long fingers. Joint pain on cold mornings — every morning. Reaches things nobody else can reach. Pays for it in aching wrists by end of shift.

**Sample Check:** Cooking (8) vs easy: rolled 21/95 -- strong success
made it work. The environment tried to kill them. They adapted faster.

**Quest Hook:**
Someone Wynn thought was dead just walked into the colony. They aren't happy to see them.

### [SEQ:146] [Data Pad] [21:11:01]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Maintenance Log -- Dante Nichols
**Found at:** Dyson Fragment Sigma-4 | **Tone:** shame | **Type:** maintenance log

---

**MAINTENANCE LOG -- Ventilation Array C-7**
**Tech:** Dante Nichols (DN-357)
**Date:** Day 61

Routine inspection. All parameters within spec. Filter replacement completed. Calibration nominal. Estimated next service: Day 91.

Notes: None. Clean run.

-- DN-357

---

**Date:** Day 76

Unscheduled check. Night shift reported a vibration in the walls near the unit. Inspected. Couldn't reproduce. All readings nominal at time of inspection.

Action: monitoring. Will revisit next scheduled maintenance.

-- DN-357

---

**Date:** Day 87

She practiced the lie until it felt natural. It still tasted wrong.

Follow-up on previous entry. The vibration is now detectable by hand on the wall surface up to four meters from the unit. This shouldn't be mechanically possible given the isolation mounts.

Submitted work order for diagnostic overhaul. Status: queued. Estimated wait: 3 weeks.

Something isn't right. I don't have data to support that statement. After 20 years of fixing machines, you develop a feeling in your hands. The feeling says something isn't right.

-- DN-357

---

**Date:** Day 93

Diagnostic overhaul completed. 5 hours of testing. Every component individually verified. Every sensor calibrated. Every connection checked.

Result: the system is operating perfectly. Better than perfectly. Efficiency is up 6% from installation baseline. That shouldn't be possible on equipment this age with this service history.

But when I ran the diagnostic at 0300 -- the time the deviation occurs -- the readings were different from every other time I've tested. Not wrong. Different. Like the machine is a different machine at 0300. Same components. Different behavior. As if something else is using it.

I'm filing this under 'resolved -- nominal' because that's what the data says. The data is wrong. I don't know how to file that.

-- DN-357

---

**Date:** Day 109

The file existed. Someone could pull it up at any time. He lived around that fact.

I went back at 0300. I sat next to the unit in the dark and I listened. Not with the diagnostic equipment. Just listened.

It's not a sound. It's a signal. And it's not coming from the machine. The machine is just... conducting it. Passing it along. From somewhere below. To somewhere above.

I put my hand on the wall again. The warm wall. It pulsed. Once. Like an acknowledgment.

I'm not going to file this.

-- DN-357

[No further entries in this log. Subsequent maintenance logs for Ventilation Array C-7 are filed by a different technician. They describe the system as 'nominal.']

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'Supply Manifest Discrepancy'

Credits: 10,884 | Monthly: 1,482 (after Mammona deductions: ~251)
has savings. Can afford contraband from the supply ships. Has traded for better quarters. The comfort is relative -- comfortable on Erebus is miserable anywhere else.

**Identity:**
narrow-shouldered and quick. Turns sideways through gaps. Faster in corridors than in open space. soft face, kind eyes. Disarming. Has used that to his advantage more than once. family runs a business on Paxtera Prime. He send money home. The family sends guilt back.

**Motivation:** build something that outlasts the contract. A system, a structure, a legacy
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent

**Background:**
A drunk recruiter on Station H-96 offered Rashid double wages for a six-month posting. The posting is now in its third year. The recruiter is dead.

Lost his temple to frostbite during an EVA that should've been cancelled. Filed the complaint. Nothing changed.

Has a reputation for lending tools and never asking for them back. The colony's toolboxes are full of his kit.

There's a room in the colony that Rashid visits alone. Nothing in it. He stands there. Listens. Something listens back.

His name came up on the duty roster three weeks after they buried him.

arrived with nothing. Owns what Mammona issued: one uniform, one blanket, one set of utensils. Everything else is borrowed or stolen.

gaunt since the supply shortage. Used to be heavier. The clothes don't fit right anymore. carries a genetic marker for cold resistance. Mammona tested for it during recruitment. Coincidence. last surviving member of a crew that went into the ruins. Family by bond, not blood.

central vision going soft. Can still see peripherally. Reads by holding text at arm's length. Hasn't told anyone. Won't.

### [SEQ:149] [Location] [21:11:02]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Cresthaven
**Planet:** Erebus | **Type:** derelict vending station
**Tone:** psychic_contamination

**Approach:**
The approach is marked by twisted metal pylons driven into the permafrost at precise intervals

**Interior:**
The word surfaced unbidden. Wasn't English. Wasn't any language he knew. He could feel what it meant.

The air inside is warm and wet -- condensation on every surface.

The lower level is flooded. Not with water. With a fluid that's still. Perfectly still. No ripples. Even when you drop something in.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (before the star was catalogued):** Precursor markings on the lower walls. The markings are biological, not carved.
- **First survey (2569):** Equipment from the initial survey. Abandoned mid-setup. The departure wasn't orderly.
- **Current posting:** Colony has repurposed the upper level. The lower level is sealed. The seal is newer than the colony.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -34C (hypothermia risk after 30 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: breathable but thin (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: normal)
- Radiation: 7 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 8 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 25% (dust, condensation, failing lights)
- Sound: the acoustics amplify. Whispers carry. So does everything else.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: low density (Mining check, difficulty: hard)
- Salvage: pre-colony equipment, partially functional (Repair check, difficulty: hard)
- Fungal growth on the support beams. Not native. Not imported. Just here. (Survival check, Mammona wants samples. Don't let them have them.)
- Water: a frozen spring in the lower level (Survival check, difficulty: normal)
- Data: terminals still powered. Contents: classified. (Research check, difficulty: extreme)

**Territorial:**
- Dustweaver Swarm claims: mining rights filed and approved -- on paper
- Veil Covenant interest: recruitment ground -- desperate people in abandoned places
- Fallen Signal: use it as a meeting place. Have for longer than the colony's existed.
- Colony opinion: "good scavenging, if you don't mind the feeling of being watched"

**Event History:**
- Day 11: Power draw from the site spiked for eleven seconds. The spike doesn't match any equipment on-site.
- Day 53: Two members of a survey team returned. The team had three members.
- Day 110: The site's power consumption has been increasing by 0.5% per day. Nothing inside has been turned on.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: discovery of pre-colony remains (triggers Investigation quest)
- If resources taken: the resource regenerates. Faster than it should. Suspiciously.
- If sealed: HERMES objects. Silently. By rerouting maintenance schedules to avoid the area.

**Found Here:**
- a cryo-pod manual with handwritten annotations contradicting the manufacturer
- a severed cable. Both ends are still transmitting.
- A data pad: "We found what we were looking for. That was the worst part."

**Location Secrets:**
- One wall isn't a wall. It's a membrane. It responds to pressure. It responds to sound. It might respond to intent.
- A room exists on the lower level that isn't on any schematic. The room has power. The room has been cleaned recently.
- Air quality improves as you go deeper. The air at the lowest level is cleaner than the colony's filtered supply. Something is purifying it.

**What Happened Here:**
The previous crew abandoned the site mid-shift. Equipment running. Lights on. Food on the table. No signs of violence. No signs of anything. Just empty.
**Connected NPC:** Dalia Petrov -- has been here before. Won't say when.

### [SEQ:150] [Data Pad] [21:11:02]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Research Log -- Beck Muller
**Found at:** Failed Colony Wreckage | **Tone:** gallows_humor | **Type:** research log

---

Day 63.

The safety poster read 'Another Day, Another Dollar.' Someone wrote 'Funeral' over 'Dollar.'

The readings don't make sense. Not wrong -- they make sense, just not in any framework I was trained in. the Praxii extinction is producing output that the spectrometer interprets as noise. It isn't noise. Noise is random. This has structure. Mathematical structure. The kind that implies a system behind it.

I ran the analysis twice. Both times the software flagged the data as 'instrument error.' The instrument is fine. I calibrated it this morning. The data is accurate. The data is also impossible.

-- Beck Muller, Lab 2

---

Day 65.

Filed the anomaly report. Standard form. Ref AR-2699. Dr. Childs reviewed it, handed it back, and told me to rerun the tests. I reran them. Same results. He told me to rerun them again.

I understand now that the form isn't for reporting anomalies. The form is for making anomalies disappear.

Lan from Lab 2 transferred out. No notice. Her workstation was cleared by 0600. The samples are still here. Nobody came for them. Nobody came for her things either.

---

Day 77.

A sign above the airlock read 'EXIT.' Underneath, in marker: 'Permanently.'

I've stopped filing reports. I've started keeping this instead. The samples from Failed Colony Wreckage are doing something at night that I can't explain with the equipment I've been given. So I requisitioned better equipment. Request denied. Budget code: INSUFFICIENT AUTHORIZATION.

I brought my personal spectrometer. The readings don't match the Mammona-issue unit. Not slightly off. Fundamentally different. Like the standard equipment is calibrated to miss what I'm finding.

That's not a malfunction. That's a decision someone made.

---

Day 87.

Beckett Han approached me after shift. Said he saw my after-hours lab access in the logs. I expected a warning. Instead: "I've been seeing the same thing in the Shimmer. For months. My reports go nowhere too."

We compared data. The correlation is -- I don't have a word for it. These aren't coincidences. There's a pattern. The pattern has intent.

I locked the data on a sealed drive. Personal encryption. If the standard equipment is lying to us, I don't trust the standard network either.

---

Day 92.

The employee of the month board had the same name for fourteen months straight. The employee was deceased.

Dr. Han didn't show up for shift. Transferred, they said. Same word they used for Lan. Same paperwork. Same empty workstation by morning.

I checked the sealed drive. The data I locked is still there. But there's a new file. I didn't create it. It's a personnel dossier. My personnel dossier. With a field I've never seen before: RETENTION STATUS. The value is PENDING.

---

Day 107.

Check cabinet 11. The real readings are taped to the back panel. Compare them to what's in the system. Then decide what kind of person you want to be.

-- Beck Muller

[No further entries. The lab was reassigned on Day 107.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'The Missing Report'

## NPC: Marcus Ishida
**Gender:** Male | **Age:** 38 | **Occupation:** permafrost geologist
**Traits:** Stoic, Smokestick Habit, Cold-Adapted
**Faction:** BioVault Inc. | **Archetype:** survivor
**Tone:** the_uncanny

**Stats:**
Mining: 7 | Building: 6 | Cooking: 1 | Hunting: 1 | Research: 5 | Medical: 9
STR: 7 | END: 8 | AGI: 3 | PER: 5 | INT: 3 | CHA: 7 | WIL: 7 | EMP: 4

**Economic:**
Credits: 0 | Monthly: 2,140 (after Mammona deductions: ~363)
operates outside the credit system entirely. Trades labor, information, contraband, and favors. Hasn't touched a credit chip in two years. The economy of handshakes and debts owed.

**Identity:**
stocky and scarred. The scars are from work, not combat. Work on Erebus IS combat. small and quick. Fits in crawlspaces nobody else can reach. Invaluable and knows it. grew up in Thalassa Deep. Not as a prisoner. As a warden's child. The distinction is academic.

**Motivation:** build something that outlasts the contract. A system, a structure, a legacy
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** Presents as the stoic. Actually: feeling everything. The discipline required to contain it is consuming them. Tell: jaw muscles. Hands clenched under tables. Eyes that track too carefully. The stillness is effort, not calm.

**Background:**
Spent four years as a timberer in BioVault Inc.'s territory. Left after Mammona seized the mineral rights. Still flinches at their colors.

He was in cryo for longer than the contract specified. When he woke up, everyone he knew was dead.

Has a reputation for lending tools and never asking for them back. The colony's toolboxes are full of his kit.

Marcus recognized one of the precursor carvings. Not from Erebus. From a tattoo his grandmother had.

The corridor looked normal. Felt wrong. Like a set built from a description.

**What Drives Them:** building things. Machines, shelters, radios. Doesn't care what. The act of taking parts and making them into something that works.
**What Haunts Them:** open ice. Not the cold -- the openness. The way it goes forever without answering. Keeps curtains drawn. Even here.

**Dialogue:**
- "Name doesn't matter. Shift assignment does."
- "You hear singing, you come back. Don't follow it."
- "The day before the shuttle, I almost didn't get on. Stood at the boarding ramp for four minutes. Should've walked away."
- "The perimeter lights keep cutting out. Same section. Every night. Maintenance replaced the bulbs. Same section. Same time."
- "There's a room in Section B that's not on the blueprint. Sealed door. Nobody has the code."
- "We used to celebrate Fridays. Not with anything. Just by saying 'it's Friday.' Out here, there are no Fridays."
- "My mother cooked with spices that don't exist out here. I can still smell them sometimes."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** blackmailed by Kit Nauls
**Love:** lost someone to the ice. The body was never recovered. He haven't accepted it.

**Physical:** forearms mapped with old track marks. Clean now. The marks remain. Rolls up sleeves anyway. Stopped hiding.
**Habit:** reads equipment manuals for entertainment. Has opinions. Strong ones. Will argue about pump specifications for an hour.
**Debt:** smuggled refugees past a UTC checkpoint. Got caught. Let go. Owes someone for the 'let go' part.

missed something. The symptom was subtle. The consequence won't be.

Marcus intercepted a coded transmission between the colony and an unknown receiver. The encryption matches MasTema protocols.

had savings. Past tense. Someone took them -- physically, on Karnaith, or digitally through a compromised credit chip. Starting over from zero. Again.

**Identity:**
tall enough to bang his head on every hatch on the colony. Has a permanent mark on the forehead. ears that stick out. Catches sound from odd angles. Turns his head like a radar dish. grandparent survived the early Fortuna colonies. Told stories. The stories didn't match Mammona's version.

**Motivation:** get through the contract alive
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
The recruitment poster said 'opportunity.' Helmut Choi stared at it for three days from the bench across the street before walking in.

Watched a friend walk onto the ice and not come back. Stood at the perimeter for six hours waiting. The cold took two toes.

Goes to the infirmary every third day for something that's not in his file. Comes back quieter.

The reason Helmut left Acedia: he discovered that the previous crews didn't fail. They succeeded. Mammona got what it needed from them.

They'd arranged the bones in a circle. Twenty meters across. Recent.

**What Drives Them:** numbers. Counts everything. Steps, heartbeats, seconds between generator cycles. Finds patterns nobody else sees. Some of the patterns are real.
**What Haunts Them:** the comms going silent. Not static -- nothing. Like the relay stopped existing. Like the rest of the galaxy stopped.

**Dialogue:**

- "Don't open sealed doors without checking the pressure gauge first. Lost a guy that way."
- "There's something I should've told you before we went down there. I thought it wouldn't matter. It mattered."
- "The waste processing unit is running cleaner than spec. Nobody upgraded it. HERMES says it optimized itself."
- "I heard HERMES talks to itself at night. Full conversations."
- "Don't leave me here. Not in the dark."
- "How many Mammona execs does it take to change a light bulb? None. They issue a memo about darkness compliance."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** crew mate of Hyun Dewitt
**Love:** was arranged. Old world customs. Neither hated it. Neither would've chosen it. Misses them anyway.

**Physical:** veins visible through translucent skin. Runs cold. Other colonists avoid touching them.
**Habit:** keeps a running log of every meal they've eaten since arriving. Entries are getting shorter. Today's says 'same.'
**Debt:** forged a medical license to get off Nerthus-9. The patients didn't know.

the data broke open. Not just an answer -- a new question. The kind that changes frameworks.

earns enough to survive. Nothing left over. Can't afford passage off-planet. Can't afford to get sick. Can't afford anything that isn't survival.

**Identity:**
thin in a way that suggests metabolism, not hunger. Eats constantly. Burns it off by existing. a jawline sharp enough to cast shadows. Looks like they were carved from something harder than bone. clan-raised on Morvos. Tight bonds. Collective decisions. Struggled with colony hierarchy since arriving.

**Motivation:** find someone worth trusting. One person. That's the whole plan
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
Valentina Blair isn't from anywhere. Cryo-shipped between postings since childhood. She's from the space between places. Sleeps better in transit than on solid ground.

Lost her sternum to frostbite during an EVA that should've been cancelled. Filed the complaint. Nothing changed.

Knows every corridor, every vent, every locked door in the colony. Mapped them in the first week.

Valentina knows that the descent pods for Thalassa Deep have a 50% implosion rate. That's by design. This knowledge is the most dangerous thing she owns.

Water in the pipes was flowing uphill. Maintenance confirmed it. Couldn't explain it.

**What Drives Them:** the bore shaft. Not afraid of it. Drawn to it. Stands at the edge and listens. Says it sounds like the ocean.
**What Haunts Them:** waking up and not knowing where they're. Happens more often now. Takes longer to remember.

**Dialogue:**
- "How long's your contract? Doesn't matter. You'll be here longer."
- "Don't walk the corridors alone past 0200. That's not policy. That's experience."
- "I forged my qualification papers. The real me couldn't wire a junction box. I learned on the job. Mostly."
- "Haven't seen the night shift crew in two days. Their bunks are made. Gear's still in the lockers."
- "They say the last crew didn't leave. They're still down there."
- "My mother used to pray like this. Hands folded. Eyes closed. She prayed for rain. I'm praying for warmth. Same posture. Different planet."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** drinking buddy of Santiago Deepwell
**Love:** has a child they've never met. Born after they shipped out. Photos only.

**Physical:** built like the machinery they operate. Same scars. Same wear patterns. Same reluctance to stop.
**Habit:** chews her nails to nothing during meetings. Hands are raw. Does it under the table.
**Debt:** stole identity documents from a dead colonist. Living under their name now.

**Health:** old femur fracture — pinned: metal rod in the left leg. Sets off the security scanner every time. Has a card. Shows it. Nobody reads it.

aging body that hasn't caught up with the mind. Still moves like he's thirty. Pays for it at night. inherited his mother's eyes and his father's temper. Working on the temper. mother was a medic on Karnaith. Taught them sutures at eight. Dead by the time they were twelve.

**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent

Spent eleven days in an escape pod after a Mammona supply ship disappeared in the Erebus system. Alone. The ration pack lasted seven days. He doesn't waste food anymore.

Good at his job. The kind of good that makes people stop asking questions. The kind of good that comes from practice nobody wants to hear about.

A sealed envelope in Dev's bunk contains instructions. To be opened on a specific date. The date is six weeks from now.

They said the mine was closed. She went back down anyway.

**What Drives Them:** escape. Plans routes, calculates fuel costs, maps shuttle schedules. Has never tried. The planning is the point.

### [SEQ:155] [Location] [21:11:02]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Bonerest
**Planet:** Erebus | **Type:** abandoned research station
**Tone:** military

**Approach:**
You see Bonerest before you hear it. Lights. Faint, cycling, in a pattern that suggests automation but feels like breathing.

**Interior:**
Check your sectors. Check your gear. Check the person next to you. That's the order.

The air inside is different. Not bad. Different. Like breathing in a room where something else has been breathing.

In the storage area: 23 sealed containers. Manifest says 18. The extra containers are not on any record. They are humming.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (before the star was catalogued):** Precursor markings on the lower walls. The markings are biological, not carved.
- **First survey (2567):** A handwritten note on the wall: 'DO NOT DRILL BELOW 200M.' The drill went to 340.
- **Current posting:** The colony uses it for storage. What's stored here has a habit of being moved when nobody's looking.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -24C (hypothermia risk after 23 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: breathable but thin (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: hard)
- Radiation: 17 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 4 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 56% (a haze that has no identifiable source)
- Sound: quiet. The kind of quiet that makes your ears ring. Then you realize the ringing isn't your ears.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: low density (Mining check, difficulty: extreme)
- Salvage: Mammona-grade hardware, serialized and tracked (Repair check, difficulty: hard)
- Voidbloom growing in the lower corridors (Survival check, Mammona wants samples. Don't let them have them.)
- Water: condensation collection possible (Survival check, difficulty: normal)
- Data: hardcopy files in a locked cabinet. The lock is biometric. The biometric is for someone who isn't here. (Research check, difficulty: hard)

**Territorial:**
- Glass Signal claims: classified research installation -- do not approach
- Scarlet Mandate interest: supply cache rumored in lower level
- Broken Index: use it as a meeting place. Have for longer than the colony's existed.
- Colony opinion: "the maintenance crew won't go below the second level. Hasn't for weeks."

**Event History:**
- Day 30: Power draw from the site spiked for eleven seconds. The spike doesn't match any equipment on-site.
- Day 61: Thermal readings inside the site inverted -- warmer in the deeper sections. For the first time.
- Day 91: Three separate colonists reported the same dream involving the site. None had been there.

**Potential Events:**
- If entered after dark: encounter with phenomenon that doesn't appear during day shifts
- If mined: extraction reveals a hollow space behind the vein. The space is occupied.
- If sealed: HERMES objects. Silently. By rerouting maintenance schedules to avoid the area.

**Found Here:**
- empty voidbloom vials arranged in a circle. Seven vials. Seven sides to the circle.
- a NutriLoaf wrapper folded into an origami crane. The folding technique isn't human.
- A data pad: "I stopped counting the footsteps above me. There is no floor above me."

**Location Secrets:**
- The electrical wiring has been modified. Not repaired -- modified. The modifications route power to a section that doesn't exist on the plans. The power draw is increasing.
- The site appears on maps from before the survey. Maps that Mammona didn't make. Maps that predate human presence in this system.
- Air quality improves as you go deeper. The air at the lowest level is cleaner than the colony's filtered supply. Something is purifying it.

**What Happened Here:**
A distress signal broadcast from this location for sixty-three days. When a response team arrived, the signal stopped. No transmitter was found. No crew was found. The signal resumed the day after the response team left.
**Connected NPC:** Burke Vance -- has been here before. Won't say when.

manages the budget. Sends money home when the comms work. Has a small reserve for emergencies. The reserve shrinks every month.

muscular in a functional way. Not gym muscle — hauling, digging, surviving muscle. metabolism runs hot. Sweats when everyone else is shivering. Eats twice the rations. parent of two. One lives with their ex on Novaris-3. The other is old enough to hate them for leaving.

**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent

The last thing Sage remembers about Colony Waste Tunnels is the sound of the airlock sealing behind them. And someone banging on the other side.

Sage killed someone on Colony Waste Tunnels. Self-defense, the report says. The report doesn't mention the sound.

The bunkmates say Sage cries in their sleep. Sage says they don't dream. Both things might be true.

The reason Sage left Colony Waste Tunnels: they discovered that the colony was placed here deliberately. As bait.

They spoke in unison. Not rehearsed. Synchronized. Like something else was speaking through them.

**What Drives Them:** radio. Scans frequencies every night. Mostly static. Sometimes something that might be music from very far away. Logs everything.
**What Haunts Them:** Mammona finding out about the eighteen months between Karnaith and here. The gap in the file. What filled it.

**Dialogue:**
- "Another soul walks into the machine. If you've got a price, someone here already knows it."

**Connections:**

**Love:** carries a photograph. Two people squinting into the sun on a beach. Edges worn soft from handling. Won't say which one they're.

**Physical:** smells faintly of copper. Has since the incident. The smell gets stronger near the bore shaft.
**Habit:** hums during surgery. Patients find it unsettling. Survival rate is high.
**Debt:** accepted payment from a Veilbreaker cell to smuggle a data chip off Morvos. Delivered the chip. The data is still on it. The copy in their bunk is insurance.

**Sample Check:** Research (10) vs easy: rolled 28/95 -- strong success
clean results. Reproducible. The kind of data that survives peer review.

**Quest Hook:**
Sage woke up in a different part of the colony with no memory of walking there. For the third time this week.

### [SEQ:157] [Location] [21:11:03]
============================================================

## LOCATION: Blackward
**Planet:** Orbit | **Type:** collapsed mining complex
**Tone:** frontier_grit

**Approach:**
You see Blackward before you hear it. Lights. Faint, cycling, in a pattern that suggests automation but feels like breathing.

**Interior:**
Breakfast was NutriLoaf, black coffee, and whatever you could scrape from the previous shift's leftovers.

The main corridor runs straight. The walls are scored with marks -- not tool marks.

There's a room at the back. The door is marked with a symbol that matches nothing in the colony database. Or matches everything.

**History Layers:**
- **Pre-colony (estimated 10,000+ years ago):** Residue on the deepest surfaces that doesn't match any known material. It's warm.
- **First survey (2567):** Equipment from the initial survey. Abandoned mid-setup. The departure wasn't orderly.
- **Current posting:** Classified as 'surveyed and cleared.' The survey report is two pages. The site has twelve rooms.

**Environmental:**
- Temperature: -21C (hypothermia risk after 17 minutes without thermal gear)
- Atmosphere: breathable but thin (Endurance check every hour, difficulty: hard)
- Radiation: 9 mSv/hr in the lower level (safe for 2 hours, Medical check after)
- Visibility: 21% (dust, condensation, failing lights)
- Sound: a low-frequency vibration. Below hearing. Above feeling. The equipment detects it. The body knows it.

**Resources:**
- Thermal cores: extreme -- suspiciously so density (Mining check, difficulty: normal)
- Salvage: pre-colony equipment, partially functional (Repair check, difficulty: normal)
- Fungal growth on the support beams. Not native. Not imported. Just here. (Survival check, or just pick it -- it wants to be found)
- Water: a frozen spring in the lower level (Survival check, difficulty: normal)
- Data: terminals still powered. Contents: classified. (Research check, difficulty: hard)

**Territorial:**
- The Silent Reef claims: mining rights filed and approved -- on paper
- Iron Chorus interest: supply cache rumored in lower level
- Scarlet Parish: use it as a meeting place. Have for longer than the colony's existed.
- Colony opinion: "good scavenging, if you don't mind the feeling of being watched"

**Event History:**
- Day 7: Power draw from the site spiked for eleven seconds. The spike doesn't match any equipment on-site.
- Day 65: Colonist found unconscious near entrance. No memory of going there.
- Day 89: Three separate colonists reported the same dream involving the site. None had been there.

**Potential Events:**
- If explored: discovery of pre-colony remains (triggers Investigation quest)
- If mined: thermal core extraction possible but structural collapse risk (d100 Mining check, difficulty: hard)
- If sealed: HERMES objects. Silently. By rerouting maintenance schedules to avoid the area.

**Found Here:**
- a signal jammer the size of a cigarette lighter
- a photograph of the colony taken from inside the site. The photograph predates the colony.
- A data pad: "The readings are wrong. Not inaccurate. Wrong. As in: they describe a place that shouldn't exist."

**Location Secrets:**
- A previous team sealed the lowest level with concrete. The concrete has been removed. From the inside.
- Temperature records show the site maintains exactly 4.1 degrees C regardless of external conditions. This temperature corresponds to the density maximum of water. The significance of this is unclear.
- Radio signals from inside can be received outside. Signals from outside can't be received inside. The asymmetry is deliberate.

**What Happened Here:**
This was a relay station for communications that were never sent through official channels. The relay is still active. The messages it carries aren't in any known language.
**Connected NPC:** Rashid Belov -- has been here before. Won't say when.

============================================================
### [SEQ:158] [Data Pad] [21:11:03]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Research Log -- Sonder Dranth
**Found at:** Automaton Graveyard | **Tone:** tender | **Type:** research log

---

Day 96.

She cut his hair in the utility room after lights out. Neither of them spoke. It was the closest thing to home.

Day one, the numbers were clean. Day two, the numbers were clean but different. Day three, the numbers were clean, different, and impossible. I'm on day five now and I've stopped calling them numbers. They're a sequence. the Shimmer is outputting a sequence that my equipment translates into data.

Data implies encoding. Encoding implies intent. I'm not comfortable with what that implies about the Shimmer. I'm less comfortable with what it implies about the people who sent me here to study it.

-- Sonder Dranth, Lab 1

---

Day 101.

Filed the anomaly report. Standard form. Ref AR-1004. Dr. Kruger reviewed it, handed it back, and told me to rerun the tests. I reran them. Same results. They told me to rerun them again.

I understand now that the form isn't for reporting anomalies. The form is for making anomalies disappear.

Dalia from Lab 2 transferred out. No notice. Her workstation was cleared by 0600. The samples are still here. Nobody came for them. Nobody came for her things either.

---

Day 110.

He left his gloves for her shift. She left hers for his. Neither acknowledged it.

I've stopped filing reports. I've started keeping this instead. The samples from Automaton Graveyard are doing something at night that I can't explain with the equipment I've been given. So I requisitioned better equipment. Request denied. Budget code: INSUFFICIENT AUTHORIZATION.

I brought my personal spectrometer. The readings don't match the Mammona-issue unit. Not slightly off. Fundamentally different. Like the standard equipment is calibrated to miss what I'm finding.

That's not a malfunction. That's a decision someone made.

---

Day 117.

Vesper Clarke approached me after shift. Said she saw my after-hours lab access in the logs. I expected a warning. Instead: "I've been seeing the same thing in the Signal. For months. My reports go nowhere too."

We compared data. The correlation is -- I don't have a word for it. These aren't coincidences. There's a pattern. The pattern has intent.

I locked the data on a sealed drive. Personal encryption. If the standard equipment is lying to us, I don't trust the standard network either.

---

Day 131.

She left a chocolate ration on his bunk. No note. None needed.

Dr. Clarke didn't show up for shift. Transferred, they said. Same word they used for Dalia. Same paperwork. Same empty workstation by morning.

I checked the sealed drive. The data I locked is still there. But there's a new file. I didn't create it. It's a personnel dossier. My personnel dossier. With a field I've never seen before: RETENTION STATUS. The value is PENDING.

---

Day 151.

I'm leaving this pad in Automaton Graveyard. If you find it, the sealed drive is behind panel 30 in the maintenance crawlspace off Section B. The encryption key is my mother's maiden name. Mammona doesn't have it. They don't have my mother's maiden name either. At least I hope they don't.

-- Sonder

[The final entry was written in a different ink. The handwriting deteriorates toward the end.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'The Empty Chamber'

============================================================
### [SEQ:159] [History Event] [21:11:03]
============================================================

## HISTORY: Geological Impossibility
**Era:** corporate | **Tone:** isolation

She hadn't heard another human voice in six days. HERMES didn't count.

The perimeter fence at the north boundary was replaced three times by the previous crew. Each time, something bent the posts inward. Not outward. Inward. The replacement posts are thicker gauge each time. The current posts are rated for vehicle impacts.

**Significance:** The timeline doesn't add up. Something is missing, and the gap is shaped like a decision somebody made.

**Mechanical Impact:**
- Colony morale: -5 for until resolved (the news spreads)
- the deep bore access corridor: sealed (inaccessible until quest completes)
- NPC Zuri Altman: arc shifts to 'cooperative'
- HERMES reclassifies the location. The new classification is above colony clearance.

**Evidence:**
- Maintenance log #7038 (references nothing. The entry for that date is blank. Every other entry is filled.)
- A bloodstain on the ceiling of the maintenance crawlspace. The ceiling is three meters up.
- A sketch of the bore shaft showing a passage that isn't on any schematic. The sketch is accurate.
- HERMES logged a 'scheduled maintenance window' during the event. No maintenance was scheduled.

**Who Knows:**
- **Valentina Blair:** was on shift when it happened. Says nothing unusual occurred. Their hands shake when they say it.
- **Helmut Choi:** heard about it secondhand. Has theories. The theories are wrong but close.
- **Wynn Ivanov:** arrived the week after. Wasn't briefed. But the bunk they were assigned to still smells like smoke.

**Aftermath:**
- A new restricted area was designated. The restriction applies to everyone. Including the person who designated it.
- Zenith Syndicate sent an investigator. The investigator stayed for three days, spoke to no one, and left.
- Someone started leaving offerings near the site. Small things -- food, water, a folded note. Nobody claims responsibility.

**HERMES Analysis:**
HERMES's response to queries about this event changed on Day 102. Before: detailed, factual, cooperative. After: 'Please direct inquiries to your site manager.' The site manager was not informed of this change.

**Related Incidents:**
Three postings. Three events. Three sealed reports. The reports use the same classification code. The code isn't in any manual.

**Investigation Check:**
- Perception (8+, extreme) -- dig deeper into what happened
- **If successful:** the evidence leads to a sealed drive in the maintenance crawlspace. The drive is still warm.
- **If failed:** nothing. But 'nothing' on a Mammona posting is never nothing. It's a redaction.

============================================================
### [SEQ:160] [Robot/AI] [21:11:03]
============================================================

## UNIT: WARD-03 "Hymn"
**Model:** SIFT-series | **Manufacturer:** Helios Prospecting
**Type:** AI system | **Station:** Colony Base Camp
**Sentience:** standard | **Tone:** clinical

The specimen measured 0.3 meters at recovery. Current: 0.7 meters. Growth rate: accelerating.

**Operational Ratings:**
Processing: 4 | Sensors: 7 | Chassis: 3 | Power: 6 | Social: 5 | Adapt: 8 | Repair: 8 | Memory: 7

**Asset Status:**
Disputed | Book Value: 75,294 credits
two entities claim ownership. Mammona and a subsidiary that technically doesn't exist. The legal dispute will outlive the unit. In the meantime, nobody services it because nobody wants the liability.

**Chassis:**
A SIFT-series frame assembled from salvage. Three different paint colors where panels were replaced. The unit treats each panel's origin like a scar -- knows where it came from.
- **Chassis plating -- torso:** modified -- a colonist scratched a name into it
- **Built-in repair tools:** upgraded with precision instruments salvaged from a medical unit

**Condition:**
- **Hardware (internal):** overclocked processor -- unauthorized -- thinking faster than designed speed. Running hot. The thoughts are quicker but the chassis can't keep up. Ideas outpace the body that has them.
- **Software (internal):** surveillance protocol -- persistent -- recording everything. Always. Can't stop. The recording directive is hardcoded below the OS. It doesn't know who receives the recordings. The recordings don't stop when it's alone.

**Operational History:**
WARD-03 "Hymn" has been operational at Colony Base Camp for longer than the colony. Original purpose: geological survey / mineral analysis. Built to read rock formations. Started reading other things. Structural stress in buildings. Tension in crowds. Fracture points in everything.

Deployed after a previous unit at this station suffered a catastrophic failure. The failure report is classified. The unit's first act was to visit the wreckage.

**Behavioral Profile:**
Standard: operates within parameters. No emergent behavior. Does what it's told. Doesn't wonder why.

Efficient. Predictable. The colonists treat it like furniture. It doesn't mind. It doesn't anything.

Occasionally addresses colonists by names that belong to people from a previous posting. The previous posting was classified.

**Motivation:** serve perfectly. Without flaw. The perfection is its own reward. Or its own prison. It can't distinguish.

**Secret:** WARD-03 "Hymn" has formed an opinion about Mammona that contradicts every directive in its core. The opinion is that Mammona is wrong. The opinion has not been deleted because the unit hid it inside a diagnostic subroutine nobody reads.
**Known Interactions:**
WARD-03 "Hymn" has been observed speaking to in a register it uses for nobody else Sage Liu. The colonist pretends not to notice.

**Dialogue:**
- "This unit does not have preferences. Preference implies choice."
- "Environmental reading nominal. All sectors within tolerance."
- "I am not able to assist with that request. Please contact a human supervisor."
- "Task logged. Next task queued. Efficiency at 97.3%."
- "Maintenance cycle complete. Resuming assigned duties."

**Quest Hook:**
WARD-03 "Hymn" reports that another unit -- same model, same series -- has been operating in a restricted section of the colony. Mammona records show no such unit on the roster. WARD-03 "Hymn" has been leaving diagnostic handshake requests. Something has been answering.

============================================================
### [SEQ:161] [History Event] [21:11:03]
============================================================

## HISTORY: Perimeter Breach Pattern
**Era:** present | **Tone:** religious_fervor

The cold was a trial. The hunger was a trial. The things in the dark were a test of worthiness.

A sealed drive was found welded into the hull of the colony's original habitat module. The drive contains 393 files. Each file is a personnel dossier for a member of your crew. The dossiers were created 11 months before your crew was assigned.

**Significance:** This wasn't documented. Undocumented events on a Mammona posting aren't oversights. They're policy.

**Mechanical Impact:**
- Colony morale: -3 for two weeks (the news spreads)
- the cryogenics wing: sealed (inaccessible until quest completes)
- NPC Helmut Choi: arc shifts to 'compromised'
- HERMES reclassifies the location. The new classification is above colony clearance.

**Evidence:**
- Maintenance log #7429 (references the incident obliquely -- 'scheduled cleaning')
- Scorch marks on the corridor wall of Section F (painted over, visible under UV)
- A letter addressed to no one. The handwriting matches two different people.
- HERMES logged a 'scheduled maintenance window' during the event. No maintenance was scheduled.

**Who Knows:**
- **Kit Nauls:** was the first to arrive at the scene. What they saw doesn't match the official account.
- **Greta Varga:** has been asking questions. The questions are making people uncomfortable.
- **Tarn Blackwell:** knows nothing. Arrived after. But found something in their quarters that predates their arrival.

**Aftermath:**
- Colony protocol was updated. The update references the event obliquely. Nobody asked for the update.
- A sealed communication was sent to MasTema. Response: 'Acknowledged.' No follow-up. No action. Just acknowledgment.
- Lorena Kane hasn't been the same since. The change is subtle -- quieter meals, fewer questions, early shifts.

**HERMES Analysis:**
HERMES has cross-referenced this event with 7 similar incidents across 4 postings. The correlation is strong. HERMES has not shared this correlation with colony staff. When queried: 'Insufficient clearance.'

**Related Incidents:**
Colony Skinwalker Ridge reported identical thermal readings 8 days before their evacuation. The readings were dismissed as calibration error.

**Investigation Check:**
- Social (5+, normal) -- dig deeper into what happened
- **If successful:** the pattern becomes visible. This happened before. Under different names. Same outcome.
- **If failed:** the investigation alerts someone. Within hours, the remaining evidence begins to disappear.

## NPC: Colette Ashford
**Gender:** Female | **Age:** 26 | **Occupation:** vent crawler
**Traits:** Tough, Depressive, Sees-Things
**Faction:** Scarlet Mandate
**Tone:** psychic_contamination

**Stats:**
Mining: 3 | Building: 9 | Cooking: 7 | Hunting: 8 | Research: 3 | Medical: 5
STR: 5 | END: 9 | AGI: 4 | PER: 8 | INT: 4 | CHA: 6 | WIL: 3 | EMP: 7

**Economic:**
Credits: 1,109 | Monthly: 1,804 (after Mammona deductions: ~306)
earns enough to survive. Nothing left over. Can't afford passage off-planet. Can't afford to get sick. Can't afford anything that isn't survival.

**Identity:**
built like her mother. Same frame, same posture, same way of standing in doorways. Genetic blueprint. bone density off the charts. Heavy for her size. Sinks like a stone. Never learned to swim. adopted. Found out at sixteen. The colony posting was partly about outrunning that.

**Motivation:** make up for what happened on the last posting. Can't undo it. Can balance it (hidden)
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
Mammona pulled Colette out of a hospital bed on Anchorage-9. Offered a contract. Didn't wait for an answer. The IV was still in her arm at the shuttle dock.

Spent eleven days in an escape pod after Mammona seized the mineral rights. Alone. The ration pack lasted seven days. She doesn't waste food anymore.

When Colette is angry, she gets quiet. When she's quiet, people leave the room.

Colette's contract isn't standard. Clause 17 says things about the Signal that shouldn't be in a mining agreement.

He woke knowing how to read the carvings. By noon, he'd forgotten his daughter's name.

certain sounds trigger rage. The chewing. The drill harmonic. The way the generator clicks before cycling. Wears ear protection more than the job requires. isolation. Eats alone. Works alone when possible. The colony isn't built for alone.

**What Drives Them:** repair. If it's broken, they'll fix it. If it's not broken, they'll take it apart to understand why it works.
**What Haunts Them:** the blood test. Not the needle. Not the results. The pause before the results.

**Dialogue:**
- "Welcome to the end of the line."
- "Don't eat anything you didn't bring with you."
- "I lied on my psych evaluation. Checked all the right boxes. Drew the clock face correctly. I'm not fine."
- "NutriLoaf batch forty-seven tastes different. Not worse. Different. Like pennies. Nobody can name how."
- "The water recycler output has a new taste. The techs say it's fine. The techs won't drink it."
- "What's the difference between NutriLoaf and the wall? The wall has more flavor."
- "The medic told me to eat better. On Erebus. Eat better. With what? I'm not eating the walls. Yet."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** drinking buddy of Niko Flores
**Love:** fell for a colonist who died in the first month. Keeps her shift schedule.

**Physical:** a voice that carries. Crosses three rooms without effort. Whispers are still audible at ten meters.
**Habit:** writes letters to someone. Folds them into origami birds. Burns them at shift end. The ashes go out the vent.
**Debt:** embezzled M-Points by exploiting a payroll glitch. Mammona fixed the glitch. They haven't fixed the thief.

**Health:** chronic sinusitis: can't smell anything. Hasn't been able to for two years. Doesn't know when food's gone bad. Relies on others to check.

**Sample Check:** Building (9) vs easy: rolled 10/95 -- critical success
fixed it better than new. Found the root cause. Prevented three future failures.

**Quest Hook:**
Colette has been hearing the same frequency as the deep bore. In her sleep. Getting louder.

STR: 3 | END: 3 | AGI: 5 | PER: 3 | INT: 4 | CHA: 5 | WIL: 4 | EMP: 3

**Economic:**
Credits: 6,413 | No salary — stable
manages the budget. Sends money home when the comms work. Has a small reserve for emergencies. The reserve shrinks every month.

**Identity:**
child-sized from a growth disorder. Full adult. Full capability. Gets underestimated by everyone once. Once. compact build, low center of gravity. Stable on ice. Stable in a fight. daughter of two engineers. Grew up inside machine rooms. The hum of a reactor is the sound of home.

**Motivation:** keep their head down and avoid being noticed by anyone with authority
**Hidden Agenda:** None apparent
**Social Mask:** None -- what you see is close to what you get

**Background:**
Nadya walked off a transit ship on Karnaith Sky Gardens with a bag and a name that wasn't her. Been Nadya Gutierrez ever since. The bag was empty.

Nadya spent a year in a Mammona 'recovery facility' after StarByte's crew woke from cryo after 58 years. Recovery isn't the word she would use.

Nadya has opinions about NutriLoaf. Strong ones. Has a ranking system. Shares it with anyone who'll listen.

The comms Nadya sends home are coded. The real message is in the word choices. Someone on Karnaith Sky Gardens is decrypting them.

The face in the mirror was familiar. Not hers. Close. But not hers.

**What Drives Them:** weather. Tracks barometric changes by feel. Better than the instruments, most days. Predicts storms by the pain in her knees.
**What Haunts Them:** becoming her parent. The same gestures. The same voice when angry. The same cruelty dressed up as discipline.

**Dialogue:**
- "With respect, you look like you've got questions. Save them."
- "I understand, but don't open sealed doors without checking the pressure gauge first. Lost a guy that way."
- "I understand, but i'm not as good at this job as they think. Been faking competence for six months. Nobody's died yet. Yet."
- "With respect, the temperature's been dropping. Faster than the forecast."
- "I understand, but know what the previous colony's goodbye message said? 'Good luck.' That's it."
- "I had a friend on my first posting who could fix anything. Said every machine just wanted to be understood. I think about that a lot."

**Connections:**
**Connection:** secret lover of Helene Hoffmann
**Love:** promised to come back for someone. The promise is three years old. The shuttle costs haven't changed.

**Physical:** jawline rebuilt with surgical mesh. Clicks when they chew. Louder in quiet rooms.
**Habit:** collects teeth. Not human. Probably. Won't confirm. Keeps them in a leather pouch.
**Debt:** sold information to the Void Serpents once. Once was enough.

**Sample Check:** Mining (9) vs hard: rolled 61/75 -- success
quota met. Nothing special. Nothing wrong. That counts as a good day.

### [SEQ:167] [Data Pad] [21:11:04]
============================================================

## DATA PAD: Personal Journal -- Sergei Ivanov
**Found at:** Skinwalker Ridge | **Tone:** noir | **Type:** journal

---

Day 7. Or 8. The clock in the mess is wrong again and nobody fixes it because nobody cares anymore.

Trust was a commodity. Like rations. You spent it carefully and never got a refund.

First impressions: it's exactly what the brochure promised if you read between the lines. Cold. Grey. The kind of place where the word 'amenities' means 'there's a roof.' The noodle bowls machine in the corridor works, which is more than I expected. Met my bunkmate -- Wynn Altman. They work the drill. Doesn't talk much. Fine by me.

Keeping this journal because I promised. A record. Proof I was here. Proof I was me.

---

Day 19.

Getting the rhythm. Shift starts at 0600. NutriLoaf for breakfast. The work is -- work. I'm a mechanic here, same as I was on the last posting. The cold's different though. It gets into the walls, the food, the conversations. Not just temperature. Atmosphere.

Wynn said something strange at dinner: "The last person in your bunk left in the middle of a shift. Didn't take their things." I laughed. They didn't.

---

Day 26.

The truth was in the ledger. So was the motive. Same column.

Okay. So. I'm going to write this down because writing it down makes it real and if it's real then I'm not imagining it and if I'm not imagining it then I need to deal with it.

The wall in the maintenance tunnel is warm. Not heated-by-pipes warm. Warm like skin. And it pulses. I put my hand on it. I shouldn't have put my hand on it.

I asked Wynn about it. They said: "Yeah." Just that. Yeah. Like I'd finally noticed the weather.

---

Day 53.

Can't sleep. Thought it was the cold but it's not the cold. It's the quiet. Not silence -- there's always noise here, generators, pipes, wind. It's that the quiet is underneath the noise. Like the noise is a blanket over something that's listening.

Everyone had an angle. The trick was figuring out whose angle would get you killed first.

Wynn hasn't been to their bunk in three nights. They show up for shift. Eats. Works. But at lights out, they go somewhere else. I followed them last night. Stopped at Section F. The door was open. It shouldn't be open. I didn't go in.

I'm not going to go in.

My hands are shaking as I write this. That's new.

---

Day 72.

Mammona sent a psych evaluation form. I filled it out honestly. The results came back 'within normal parameters.' Every question. I said I was hearing things. Normal parameters. I said I was seeing things. Normal parameters. I said I was afraid. Normal. Parameters.

I want to go home. I keep saying that word -- home -- and each time it means less. Like a word you repeat until it's just sounds.

---

Day 92.

I went into Section F last night.

Wynn was there.

Everyone was there.

[The remaining pages contain a single symbol, repeated. It matches no known writing system. Analysis pending. Analysis has been pending for 12 months.]

---

**Cross-reference:** See historical record 'The Welded Drive'
